#5143. Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

Binance Hack Launderers Mint Elon Musk and Animal Meme Coins to Move Stolen Crypto

North Korea’s hackers have found a novel use for the internet’s favorite joke tokens: laundering a slice of the record $6.4 billion stolen from Binance.

According to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, the launderers are creating their own worthless meme coins and using them to shuffle stolen funds through decentralized exchanges in a way that breaks the direct onchain link between the thief’s wallet and the one collecting the proceeds.

The launderers mint a brand-new token, pair it with a real asset like Near or a stablecoin in a liquidity pool, pay stolen funds into the pool through one set of wallets to buy the token, then sell it back through a separate set of wallets to withdraw clean-looking assets.

Because the pool sits in the middle, no stolen funds move directly from the buying wallet to the collecting one, and the receiving wallets look like ordinary traders cashing out a meme coin.

Elliptic, in research credited to co-founder Tom Robinson, said the launderers deployed the tactic on Tron and Near, with a close variant on Solana. The clearest example came on Near, where between Nov. 5 and 7 they created at least six home-made tokens, each paired with Near in its own pool.

The coins carried the usual meme-coin trappings: cartoon logos of a bow-wearing monkey, a flag-draped duck, a chimpanzee and a fox, one even featuring an “Elon 2028 President” image of Elon Musk, who has no connection to the tokens. The names ran from the crude (FUK) to a riff on dog coins (SINU).

Each token cost about 25 Near tokens, roughly $90, to create. All six followed the same arc: launched, traded heavily within hours using stolen NEAR, then abandoned, with the leftover liquidity pulled on Nov. 8.

In total, about 117,000 Near, or roughly $430,000, passed through the six tokens before much of it flowed into stablecoins or a cross-chain bridge.

The sums are modest so far, a reminder that the bulk of the haul is still parked onchain. Elliptic earlier found more than $3 billion of the stolen crypto had yet to be laundered, and that the launderers were spinning up a new wallet roughly every 25 seconds to move funds at machine speed.

Crucially, the meme-coin trick doesn’t actually hide the money. Elliptic stressed the trail remains fully traceable onchain; the maneuver only slows investigators down, buying time in what is essentially a race to move funds before they get flagged.

It’s one cheap, disposable layer among many that North Korea’s launderers stack together. Elliptic first observed the tactic last year while tracing the $1.5 billion Bybit theft, also attributed to the TraderTraitor group behind the Binance breach.

For compliance teams, the firm warned, funds arriving from a liquidity pool shouldn’t be assumed clean, especially where freshly minted tokens and a handful of trading wallets are involved.

#5140. Ripple-Backed Evernorth Plunges 40% as Insiders Move to Sell 73.7 Million Shares

Ripple-backed XRP treasury company Evernorth Holdings (XRPN) is under extreme pressure as its shares have declined by over 40% while big investors like Ripple Labs move closer to potentially selling millions of shares.

Earlier this week, Evernorth filed a prospectus with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), indicating that the XRP-focused company has registered a whopping 73,688,162 Class A shares for potential resale by existing shareholders.

Evernorth's prospectus comes as the XRP-focused company is enduring a brutal stock market debut. XRPN began trading on Nasdaq on Oct. 12.

On Nov. 10, Evernorth's stock price dropped 23.54%. XRPN ended the day at $7.96, compared to $10.41 in the previous trading session. Moreover, about 1.39 million shares were traded, which is a significant jump from about 369,000 shares on Nov. 9.

So far, Evernorth's share price has declined by around 48% from its closing price on Oct. 28 ($15.26). The magnitude of the decline is even greater if we consider the high share price on its debut day ($19.39). XRPN's stock price is down by around 59% from that high.

Some of the biggest names and companies linked to the XRP ecosystem are shareholders who have registered shares for potential sale, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

For instance, the biggest shareholder is Arrington XRP Capital Fund, which was a sponsor of the merger between Evernorth and Armada Acquisition Corp. II. It has about 33.82 million shares registered for possible resale.

Similarly, the second-largest shareholder is Ripple Labs, which has about 18.14 million shares registered. Then there are various entities linked to SBI Holdings, a big Japanese financial institution. They have 11.79 million shares registered, as per the prospectus filed with the SEC.

The Larsen Lam Children's Remainder Trust, which is linked to Ripple co-founder Chris Larsen, has about 7.15 million shares registered for possible sale.

Other names mentioned in the filing include Pantera Capital, GSR Markets Switzerland and Kraken parent firm Payward.

It is important to understand that the fact that they've registered shares for sale doesn't mean that these investors have sold their holdings or necessarily plan to sell them.

For example, Ripple, the Arrington-sponsored entity and a Larsen-linked entity are all subject to six-month lock-up agreements following the completion of Evernorth's business combination on Oct. 9.

These lock-up agreements are typically set to expire on April 9, 2027, with some exceptions.

#5139. Buying Bitcoin without betting on a rally may not pay

A November 10 snapshot offered about $119 per Bitcoin from a hedged trade, against $135 in illustrative borrowing costs over 17 days.

Buying Bitcoin while hedging against a price drop can earn a return without a rally. But November 10 pricing offered about $119 per BTC before costs, less than the roughly $135 needed to finance the purchase for 17 days in an illustrative borrowing calculation.

The strategy, known as cash-and-carry, pairs a Bitcoin purchase with a futures sale. It seeks to earn the premium between the two markets as their prices converge. Its attraction depends on the cost of funding and maintaining both positions.

The comparison uses CME’s November futures settlement and Coinbase’s price during the settlement window. It implies about 3.48% gross annualized carry, against a 3.88% overnight financing benchmark.

CME’s preliminary bulletin No. 217, dated November 10, lists the November Bitcoin futures settlement at $73,805 per BTC.

Coinbase’s BTC-USD candle beginning at 20:59 UTC closed at $73,685.73. That minute corresponds to 2:59–3:00 p.m. Central Standard Time, aligning with CME’s stated daily settlement window.

Subtracting the Coinbase close from the futures settlement gives a $119.27 premium, or about 0.16% of the spot price. Using the 17-day calendar-date difference to November 27 expiry and a simple 365-day annualization produces the following sensitivity:

The range shows how much a short-dated annualized figure can move with a relatively small change in spot price.

These remain pricing proxies. Coinbase’s candle close is a single venue’s last trade within the minute, while CME’s daily settlement methodology uses a volume-weighted futures price. A trader would need executable prices for both legs, at sufficient size, to establish an actual opportunity. Pairing the minute’s lowest spot trade with the futures settlement does not demonstrate that both prices were simultaneously available.

CME’s own BasisWatch methodology uses a separate 60-second spot marker.

The New York Fed’s dated SOFR observations show 3.88% effective November 9. SOFR measures overnight borrowing secured by Treasury securities. A Bitcoin trading desk’s funding rate, collateral requirements and access to credit can differ materially.

There is also a convention difference. The carry calculation above uses a 365-day year. The New York Fed’s money-market methodology uses actual calendar days divided by 360.

For illustration, financing the entire $73,685.73 spot purchase for 17 days at a constant 3.88%, using that 360-day convention, would cost approximately $135.01. Against the $119.27 gross premium, that leaves a $15.74 shortfall before trading and other costs.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Using the minute’s low instead would leave roughly $21 per BTC after the same financing assumption, before other costs. That is a small remaining allowance, and it depends on a more favorable spot input than the closing-price proxy.

Future overnight rates, borrowing spreads, execution costs and collateral income remain unknown. An investor using its own cash would face an opportunity-cost decision rather than this hypothetical loan bill.

Margin adds another demand on capital. CME futures are marked to market daily. If Bitcoin rises, the short futures position can require cash payments even while the spot holding gains value elsewhere. The investor needs sufficient liquidity to maintain the hedge.

The 3.48% figure measures gross carry against the spot purchase price. Return on margin uses a different denominator again, and looking only at the futures deposit would omit the capital supporting the long position.

An ETF version also requires its own calculation using share prices and hedge ratios. Fund fees and premiums or discounts to net asset value affect the economics. For example, IBIT lists a 0.25% annual sponsor fee; buying its shares would not reproduce the Coinbase spot leg exactly.

The macro backdrop gives financing costs particular relevance. The October CPI release, published November 10, showed consumer prices rising 0.4% month over month, with core prices up 0.3%, both seasonally adjusted.

Before the release, Governor Christopher Waller said a core reading around 0.3% or higher would warrant considering a 50-basis-point December rate increase. Those were his conditional views, rather than a committee decision.

The November futures contract expires before the Fed’s December 8–9 meeting. A December increase therefore would not become an already-incurred financing cost on a position completed at November expiry. Policy expectations can still affect present funding conditions and the economics of rolling into later contracts.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $95 million in net outflows on November 10, following $201 million on November 9, according to Farside’s completed daily rows.

Those totals establish continued aggregate redemptions. They do not identify investors’ strategies or connect a particular ETF holding to a futures hedge.

The available CFTC financial futures report covers positions as of November 3, a week before the CPI session. It cannot establish how traders responded to November 10 prices. Its trader categories also lack a complete account-level view of corresponding spot and ETF holdings.

Evidence consistent with new carry demand would combine executable futures premiums that leave room after actual funding costs, additional spot or ETF exposure, and corresponding futures hedges. Comparable pricing across subsequent sessions would help distinguish a persistent incentive from a fleeting observation.

Bitcoin can also recover through stronger directional spot demand from buyers willing to take outright price risk. Cash-and-carry is one possible source of support, with its own financial hurdle.

For this hedged Bitcoin trade, the buying decision turns on the premium left after funding, execution and the cash needed to keep both positions open.

#5138. Google Takes Aim at Nvidia With New AI Chips Built for Privacy and Massive Memory

The TPU 8tx and TPU 8i Max join Google’s eighth-generation Tensor Processing Unit lineup, giving businesses four configurations for training and running AI models. Google says it serves nearly 100 TPU customers, from AI labs to universities.

Unlike Nvidia’s widely used graphics processing units, TPUs are purpose-built AI accelerators. Google developed them for its own services but increasingly sells access to outside companies, including on-premises customers.

“With computing moving from human to agent scale, businesses are asking for accelerators that can reason across AI models’ massive context windows,” wrote Amin Vahdat, Google’s chief technologist and senior vice president of AI and infrastructure, in Google's announcement.

TPU 8tx targets a problem that many serious businesses have when it comes to adopting AI: protecting sensitive information while a model is using it.

Data is encrypted in storage and during transmission, but processing typically requires decrypting it in memory. Google says the configuration uses hardware-level confidential computing to isolate customer data and proprietary model parameters even during operation.

The system combines TPU 8t silicon with fifth-generation AMD EPYC processors and Google’s low-latency Boardfly network. Google claims it can cut latency by up to half versus TPU 8t, with almost no performance penalty for the added protection.

Companies could fine-tune and run models on the same system instead of transferring sensitive information between clusters. However, confidential computing cannot prevent a poorly designed AI agent from mishandling data it is allowed to access.

TPU 8i Max addresses another limitation: memory. Autonomous agents analyzing data, codebases, documents or hours of video, etc; need fast access to far more information than conventional AI tasks.

Google combines two TPU 8i chips into one package, doubling computing power, on-chip SRAM and high-bandwidth memory throughput versus TPU 8i. The design packs twice as much silicon into the same rack footprint.

“Our fourth system, TPU 8i Max, is built for a different problem: the memory wall,” Vahdat wrote.

Google is open-sourcing TPU Sync, software that moves AI working memory between chips, servers and storage. It claims the system can reduce certain data-transfer delays and response startup times by more than 90%.

Google's announcement comes as Nvidia faces mounting pressure on Wall Street. Shares plunged 9.3% on Thursday and fell another 5% in Monday's trading after CEO Jensen Huang admitted to an extramarital affair with a woman suspected by U.S. intelligence of spying for China. Nvidia's board has launched an independent investigation, although the company says it has no evidence that corporate secrets were compromised.

The scandal adds to concerns about Nvidia's growth as competitors target its AI chip dominance. OpenAI is reportedly planning to purchase Google's TPUs to reduce its reliance on Nvidia. This is still unconfirmed, but Google's expanding chip lineup could give AI developers more bargaining power, threatening Nvidia's market share and future growth expectations.

Google is competing on specialized hardware and software, not just chip speed. Its open-source TorchTPU project also lets developers run PyTorch and Hugging Face models on TPUs without rewriting their applications.

Still, Google’s performance figures compare its new systems with earlier Google hardware, not Nvidia’s latest products. The company did not disclose pricing or availability dates for the two new configurations.

#5134. Solana (SOL), Gram (GRAM), Hyperliquid (HYPE) and Bitcoin (BTC) Price Analysis for November 12: It Is a Bloodbath on the Market

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Solana is currently trading at $83.66. The price is back in the area where its August rally started.

The fall in Solana's price that we are witnessing now is not a normal correction. The price has lost more than 30% of its value since September, when it traded at $125. Usually, corrections stop at around 10% for volatile assets, including cryptocurrencies. Here, the situation is different.

By the end of October, Solana dropped below the black moving average on the chart, which represents the 200-day moving average, suggesting a long-term change of direction. The short-term moving averages crossing below the longer-term ones suggest the bearish trend is getting worse.

The upcoming support is at approximately $80, with a lower support at $72-$78. That range is where SOL spent the whole of July, before breaking out in mid-August. The $80 level is unlikely to hold under such severe pressure, so traders shouldn't be surprised to see SOL trading around $70.

With the spike in trading volume on November 10, we pretty much have confirmation that the downtrend is continuing.

Gram is trading at $1.244 after briefly dipping below $1 on November 9.

Fueled by Pavel Durov's comments on X, Gram received a substantial boost: 24% in three days, with the most recent daily candle showing a 9% surge.

The bounce started thanks to the Telegram wallet updates and the further integration of blockchain technology directly into the messenger, along with speculative buying. Durov states that Telegram users created more than 700,000 Gram wallets in the last 48 hours.

Record-breaking volume on November 10 could reflect concentrated buying, meaning that someone or some group targeted GRAM and put funds into it directly.

The price has already moved above the short green moving average, at around $1.21. Unfortunately, resistance is quite steep: the October-November trailing resistance is at around $1.25. Other moving averages present a chance for bears to play around $1.34 and $1.46.

In late September, GRAM spiked towards $1.73, but failed to hold that momentum and quickly tumbled back below the black moving average.

The current bounce is mixed: both speculative and fundamental factors are fueling it, so its future is somewhat unclear.

Despite positive developments around Hyperliquid, such as listings on South Korean exchanges on November 10 and active buybacks, HYPE is diving. The asset lost around 14% over the week.

Additionally, Hyperliquid saw a bounce to $97 after the October 29 announcement about Hyperliquid-based futures in the US. Unfortunately, it hasn't helped, with HYPE down almost $25 since then.

From a price perspective, the only positive outcome is the breakthrough of the 200 EMA back in August, which might become support at some point, though that has not happened yet.

Currently, Hyperliquid is stuck in the $70-$73 range. It seems unlikely to break out until it hits the 200 EMA at around $68 and bounces off it.

Bitcoin is trading at $73,535, down 16% from its September peak. Unfortunately, the market is brutal right now and clearly shows a serious level of pressure.

Bitcoin spot ETFs have lost around $1.6 billion since the beginning of November, though the dynamic is improving. According to Bloomberg's Eric Balchunas, only stock funds saw the biggest inflows, which suggests a continuing trend of outflows from digital gold in favor of more traditional assets.

A better time to buy Bitcoin could be tied to an overall market recovery, and therefore to a better market catalyst, which doesn't exist at this point.

The Anthropic IPO, growing demand for memory chips, and the rapid improvement of AI push investors away from the cryptocurrency industry, which has been in pain for the last two years, lacking catalysts, safety and use cases.

#5132. Binance hacker’s XRP stockpile rapidly crashes driven by Bitcoin swaps

Stolen XRP conversions into Bitcoin left about 60 million XRP at Elliptic’s Nov. 10 cutoff, while refunds and swap access complicate further selling.

The XRP stockpile linked to Binance's October hack had shrunk to about 60 million tokens by Nov. 10, according to blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, after another round of conversions largely into Bitcoin.

Elliptic's Nov. 10 update reported about 35 million additional XRP swapped through THORChain overnight, almost all into Bitcoin.

The change comes during broader XRP weakness, which is down 24% over the previous 30 days. The laundering figures do not establish if that decline resulted from the attacker’s conversions.

Bitquery's theft trace identified 233.5 million XRP sent from five Binance accounts during the Oct. 29 attack.

Elliptic put the remaining balance at about 192 million XRP on Nov. 6, about 148 million in its Nov. 7 account, and about 95 million by early afternoon Nov. 9. Its Nov. 10 estimate was about 60 million, including roughly 33 million in an address that had remained unmoved since its Nov. 2 funding.

Rounded XRP remaining: 192 million on November 6 at 08:45 UTC; 148 million on November 7, time unstated; 95 million by early afternoon November 9; 60 million at 11:00 UTC November 10, 2026. These are dated balances, not net completed-sale totals.

Source: Elliptic update, November 10, 2026, 11:00 UTC

Elliptic also described repeated interruptions, saying that THORChain halted XRP trading on Nov. 7, with swaps stopping for almost a day, and reported successful swaps again from Nov. 8 afternoon. On the morning of Nov. 10, it said XRP swaps appeared halted again, with new deposits refunded.

THORChain's documentation says a trading halt stops swaps while allowing refunds, a signing halt queues outbound payments, and a full chain halt stops observation of that chain. A pending payment, a returned XRP deposit, and a completed Bitcoin conversion represent different outcomes.

Bitint's Nov. 9 reconstruction put XRP actually converted at no more than 93.85 million for its defined deposit population after refund corrections. Its Bitcoin settlement ledger cut off at Nov. 7, and was rechecked through Nov. 8.

THORChain's streaming-swap mechanism breaks an order into smaller parts that can reduce slippage, the price cost of executing against available liquidity. When parts are spaced apart, arbitrageurs can rebalance the pools between them.

The protocol also tracks successful and unsuccessful amounts separately. A stream can pay out the converted value and return the unsuccessful XRP portion, while a failed first attempt can trigger a full refund.

Counting the deposit, its smaller executions, and its payout as separate sales would overstate activity.

Execution, refunds and outbound settlement answer different questions. Explore the protocol outcomes behind a wallet departure.

Successful parts pay out Bitcoin; unsuccessful parts return XRP. The deposit amount is not the converted amount.

If the first streaming attempt fails, the entire XRP deposit can be refunded. A wallet departure need not become a sale.

A signing halt queues outbound payments. Execution and receipt of the payout are separate steps.

Match successful execution with payouts and refunds before counting converted XRP.

Qualitative protocol diagrams from the retained THORChain halt and streaming documentation. Paths show possible asset and settlement states, not amounts or probabilities.

Protocol mechanics only, not a chronology of attacker transactions or a statement of current route status. No throughput, proportions or attacker-specific streaming settings are established.

The next useful observations are fresh balances in the remaining addresses, matched successful XRP conversions and Bitcoin payouts, and the route's actual refund or halt state.

Smaller inventory limits what remains on this tracked leg, and usable liquidity and access govern how quickly it can be converted.

In its Nov. 10 update, Elliptic estimated that a negligible amount had likely been cashed out across the wider laundering operation: much of the activity had changed the asset held while leaving funds on public blockchains.

Selling XRP for Bitcoin reduces that XRP exposure even while the proceeds remain in crypto.

#5131. AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI Data Centers Are Buying Used Jet Engines as New Power Turbines Take Six Years

AI data-center developers are hunting for used gas turbines and retired jet engines because ordering brand-new power equipment could leave their servers waiting for electricity until the next decade.

Some companies are buying refurbished machines, while others are converting aircraft-engine technology into generators that can run alongside their computing facilities.

New gas turbines now take an average of six years to deliver in the United States, according to energy consultancy Wood Mackenzie. Delivery discussions are already focused on 2030 and 2031, a timeline that clashes with an AI industry trying to bring enormous computing campuses online much sooner.

Global turbine orders have topped 100 gigawatts, compared with manufacturers' estimated annual capacity of just 60 to 70 gigawatts, Reuters reported. Siemens Energy, GE Vernova, and Mitsubishi Heavy Industries have all said orders are outpacing what they can produce.

Gas turbines can generate large amounts of electricity by burning natural gas to spin equipment connected to a generator. AI data centers need that power around the clock to run densely packed chips and cooling systems, and connecting new facilities to public electricity grids can itself take years.

The International Energy Agency expects global data-center electricity consumption to nearly double from 485 terawatt-hours in 2025 to 950 in 2030. Consumption at AI-focused facilities is projected to triple over the same period.

One way around the backlog is an aeroderivative turbine, a power generator built using technology adapted from aircraft jet engines.

Other companies are going further by seeking older machines and retired aircraft engines that can be rebuilt for stationary power production. For example, AI infrastructure firm Crusoe ordered 13 units from Missouri-based ProEnergy in April, each rated at 50 megawatts, for a combined 650 megawatts. The jet-engine-derived equipment is intended for future U.S. data centers, but the contract doesn’t mean that Crusoe bought engines removed from retired aircraft.

Second-hand machines can also carry a substantial discount. Used turbines in good condition sell for about $1.5 million to $1.7 million per megawatt, compared with roughly $2 million to $2.5 million for new units, according to equipment brokers and energy analysts interviewed by Reuters.

One broker described a sale of two smaller turbines totaling 29 megawatts for $12.5 million. Equivalent new equipment would have cost about $35 million and taken five years to arrive, he said.

“That secondary market is very, very hot right now,” EthosEnergy CEO Ana Amicarella told Reuters, adding that asking prices are climbing as more buyers compete for the same machinery.

Building a power plant beside a data center also brings fuel needs, air emissions, and permits into the equation. The Environmental Protection Agency says stationary turbines and engines used by data centers are subject to federal air-pollution standards.

Last week, OpenAI promised to cover electricity and grid costs at future campuses it owns and operates after voters backed tighter restrictions on data-center construction.

Power-market research firm Cleanview identified about 90 gigawatts of proposed on-site generation at U.S. data centers in its mid-2026 analysis. Only about 2 gigawatts was operating at the time

Developers are looking beyond turbines, too. Finnish manufacturer Wärtsilä told Reuters it had booked around 3 gigawatts of orders from U.S. data centers since spring 2025 for generators powered by gas engines rather than turbines.

#5129. Bitcoin faces $70,000 test as $14.2 billion stablecoin contraction exposes demand weakness

Bitcoin demand has contracted by 284,000 BTC as stablecoin supply shrinks and recent buyers face losses that could complicate a recovery.

Bitcoin is facing a fresh liquidity squeeze as mounting losses among recent buyers threaten to derail its recovery.

Stablecoin supply has contracted by $14.2 billion over the past month, its sharpest decline since June 2022, while Bitcoin's apparent demand has fallen by 284,000 BTC, approaching levels last seen during the March 2025 market turmoil.

The deterioration has left Bitcoin vulnerable to another decline toward $70,000, with investors who purchased at higher prices potentially adding to selling pressure if a recovery fails to attract fresh capital.

The retreat in stablecoin supply marks a sharp reversal from the conditions that supported Bitcoin's previous rally.

According to Glassnode's Nov. 11 report, the aggregate supply of dollar-linked tokens declined by $14.2 billion over 30 days, the fastest contraction since the 2022 market crisis.

30-day change in supply of major USD stablecoins, with unresolvable points omitted. Historical readings are approximate monthly and selected event-date samples digitized from the Glassnode chart, rounded to $0.5B.

Source: Glassnode Week On-chain, Nov. 11, 2026 (through Nov. 9) · Glassnode original stablecoin supply chart screenshot · Glassnode 30-day stablecoin supply net position change metric

This corroborates CryptoQuant's earlier research, which showed that Tether's USDT had lost $6.6 billion in market capitalization over 60 days, more than twice the $3.2 billion decline recorded following FTX's collapse in December 2022.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The reversal is particularly striking against the liquidity expansion of the previous two years. USDT's 60-day supply growth peaked at $20.6 billion in December 2024 and generally ranged between $10 billion and $16 billion during much of 2025.

The contraction has coincided with a sharp deterioration in Bitcoin's apparent demand.

CryptoQuant's apparent demand indicator fell to negative 284,000 BTC over 30 days, its weakest reading since March 28, 2025. The measure estimates the difference between newly issued and newly active supply and the amount the market absorbs.

Comparable readings of negative 309,000 BTC in March 2025 and negative 273,000 BTC in June 2026 occurred during earlier market downturns followed by recoveries.

Bitcoin subsequently recovered from its June lows even as the Coinbase Premium Index remained predominantly negative, suggesting the indicator alone was insufficient to capture the rebound's strength.

However, the current downturn has unfolded alongside persistent institutional selling and a broader withdrawal of dollar liquidity.

CryptoQuant found that the Coinbase Premium Index remained negative for 30 consecutive days, averaging approximately minus 0.14%. Glassnode's adjusted measure separately recorded its deepest 30-day discount since February.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds have also recorded $1.6 billion in net outflows across 20 trading sessions.

That combination leaves Bitcoin increasingly dependent on buyers willing to absorb supply without the additional support previously provided by expanding stablecoin issuance and ETF inflows.

The liquidity contraction is occurring as investors who entered the market during the August and September rally increasingly find themselves holding Bitcoin at a loss.

CryptoQuant data shows that the unrealized profit margin of coins held for one to three months has collapsed from 33% on Sept. 22 to negative 6%. The 39% reversal has left these investors with an average acquisition price near $79,500, against Bitcoin's market price of approximately $74,600 when the research was compiled.

Previous corrections during the current cycle have frequently pushed this profitability measure toward negative 12% before stabilizing. More severe downturns drove it to negative 18% during July to September 2024, negative 19% in April 2025, and negative 23% in July 2026.

At the latest $79,500 acquisition price, a negative 12% margin would put Bitcoin near $70,000, roughly 6% below CryptoQuant's reference price.

Glassnode's broader short-term holder data reveals that only 35% of Bitcoin held by investors who acquired their coins within the past 155 days remains profitable, compared with nearly all such supply at September's high.

Their aggregate cost basis stands near $75,600, while substantial concentrations of Bitcoin purchased between August and October sit at prices extending to $83,600.

A recovery toward those acquisition levels could encourage investors to exit at breakeven, adding to selling pressure in a market already struggling to attract fresh capital.

Oluwapelumi values Bitcoin's potential. He imparts insights on a range of topics like DeFi, hacks, mining and culture, underlining transformative power.

#5127. Bitmine reports $6.3 billion net loss in latest fiscal year, driven by 44% ether decline

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

According to its Form 10-K filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission, nearly $6.23 billion of the net losses came from unrealized fair value losses on digital asset holdings.

Bitmine Immersion Technologies, Inc. Bitmine Immersion Technologies, Inc. (BMNR) reported a net loss of more than $6.3 billion for the fiscal year ended Aug. 31, 2026, due to a significant decline in the value of ether Ethereum (ETH). In the prior fiscal year, the firm reported a net income of $348.6 million.

According to Form 10-K filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission, nearly $6.23 billion of the net losses came from unrealized fair value losses on digital asset holdings.

As of the end of August, the Tom Lee-led Ethereum treasury firm held 5,901,837 ETH with a fair value of $14.6 billion. It also held 211 BTC Bitcoin (BTC) at a fair value of $16.6 million. Ether dropped around 44% in the fiscal year, falling from approximately $4,390 to $2,466.

"Because our digital assets are measured at fair value, with changes in fair value recognized in earnings, changes in ETH prices are expected to continue to cause significant volatility in our reported results, stockholders’ equity and financial condition," the company said in the filing.

During the fiscal year, the company sold 395.1 million shares of common stock through its at-the-market offering program, raising $13.2 billion in gross proceeds. It also repurchased 20.8 million shares at an average price of $17 per share.

Bitmine further said that revenue from staking and validation accounted for 97% of its total revenue, adding that the decline in ETH's value also adversely affected staking revenue, as rewards are received in the cryptocurrency. The firm reported $124.8 million in yearly revenue during the period.

Despite significant paper losses reflecting Ethereum's value decline, Bitmine continues to purchase more of the cryptocurrency. According to its Monday press release, Bitmine said it has accumulated 6,070,092 ETH, reaching 4.97% of the token's supply. The company's chairman, Tom Lee, said that Bitmine would stop accumulating once it reaches the "Alchemy of 5%" target it set when its Ethereum treasury strategy in June 2025.

#5126. Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic's Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.0 Are Impressive, But Be Prepared to Sell a Kidney to Afford Them

Anthropic released Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 on Monday, promising AI models that can turn a handful of instructions into working software, design physical objects, and tackle research problems that previously required teams of specialists.

You can now ask a chatbot to build the app you've always wanted. You may also need to sell a kidney to pay for it.

That's not because every prompt costs thousands of dollars. It's because Fable can keep working for days, writing code, testing it, and fixing its own mistakes while the meter keeps running.

Some users have already tested its capabilities, and they’ve been impressed—by the power, and the price.

AI YouTuber Matthew Berman put the model through a series of ambitious tests and described Fable 5.2 as the “best AI model” he's ever used. His projects included playable video games and a browser-based version of Microsoft Excel, complete with formulas and pivot tables.

One single level of Gears of War: E-Day-inspired level took 43 hours and 48 minutes of build time and processed 8.63 billion tokens—small chunks of information an AI reads and produces. The running API cost estimate, based on pay-as-you-go pricing for developers, had reached $14,710.

I've burned over 100 billion tokens early testing Fable 5.2. It is BY FAR the best model I've ever used.

Here's my full review (including Gears of War clone):

> Fable 5.2 is the most autonomous model to date. Given a task with little direction, it makes… pic.twitter.com/0xTzqTY040

— Matthew Berman (@MatthewBerman) November 9, 2026

The Excel clone ran up an estimated $11,700 after discounts for cached inputs, or information the system can reuse instead of processing from scratch. Berman also showed a Fall Guys-style game that consumed more than 9 billion tokens over 71 hours, equivalent to roughly $13,000.

Those are estimated API costs, and the projects weren't flawless: Berman reported awkward animations, bugs, and features that still needed testing.

If you’re poor, well… Fable also knows how to work for cheap. For less than $40 (two months of Claude Pro) it built a cute orange cylinder.

Another experiment simulated water-balloon physics for $91. It’s worth noting this isn’t a model intended for routine tasks. This is for serious work and people with deep pockets.

In its launch announcement, Anthropic said the new models generate output up to 30% faster than their predecessors. The company also says Fable helped solve two open theoretical-physics problems involving black holes and design three functioning electronic devices, claims that will need independent scrutiny.

Anthropic's internal FrontierCode 2.0 evaluation, which measures the percentage of proposed coding changes that would be accepted into real open-source projects, put Fable 5.2 at 49.1%, compared with 32.4% for Fable 5.1. That helps explain why users are pushing it beyond simple chat responses.

Fable and Mythos share the same underlying model, but Fable has stricter safeguards around dangerous biological and cybersecurity requests. Mythos is reserved for verified organizations working in those fields.

The standard API price is $8 per million input tokens and $40 per million output tokens, down from the previous generation's $10 and $50. Optional fast mode can generate output up to 2.5 times faster, but doubles the rates to $16 and $80.

For contrast, Kimi 3.1 Pro, a model considered by many as the best Chinese AI model costs $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens. The latest Deepseek model costs $0.6 and $1.98 outside of peak hours.

Per Anthropic’s standards, Fable’s price is still twice the per-token price of Claude Opus 5.5, which Anthropic recommends for everyday work. Its flagship is meant for problems the cheaper models struggle to finish, not for asking whether your email sounds too aggressive.

Just last week, speculation swirled around a supposed Fable 5.5. The actual release is 5.2, with cheaper token rates but enough appetite to turn a hobby project into a serious expense.

Anthropic, last we checked, doesn’t accept kidneys as a method of payment.

#5123. Bitcoin whales stack coins while ETF investors dump millions daily

Bitcoin wallets holding 100 to 10,000 BTC resume accumulation as US spot ETFs record another $95 million in outflows.

Bitcoin's latest accumulation signal is concentrated in two large-wallet groups, while US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) continue to record withdrawals.

In its Nov. 11 report, on-chain analytics firm Glassnode identified accumulation in the 100-1,000 BTC and 1,000-10,000 BTC cohorts over the seven-day period through Nov. 9.

These cohorts, grouped by the amount of Bitcoin held, both recorded scores above 0.5. The analytics firm said every other wallet-size cohort was still reducing aggregate holdings, including the largest group above 10,000 BTC and the smallest below 1 BTC.

The newer ETF figures extend that picture through Nov. 10, when the funds recorded net withdrawals of $95 million, according to Farside Investors.

Glassnode said both had spent much of October distributing before returning to accumulation. Their behavior suggests some large holders are adding to their balances, even as participation elsewhere remains weak.

The report added that the same two cohorts turned to accumulation in late September, but the episode did not persist.

Glassnode's seven-day observations through November 9 show two large-wallet groups accumulating while the smaller and largest groups reduce holdings.

Both groups had spent much of October distributing.

Both groups turned to accumulation, but that earlier episode did not persist.

Participation remains narrow, and the same two groups' late-September accumulation did not persist.

A score near 1 indicates stronger weighted accumulation, while a reading near 0 can indicate distribution or a lack of accumulation.

The Bitcoin balance bands for wallets do not establish beneficial ownership or show whether the holders are institutions, individuals, or buyers using a particular funding source.

Farside's completed November sessions for spot Bitcoin ETFs show withdrawals in six of the seven trading days from Nov. 2 through Nov. 10. Withdrawals resumed after the Nov. 4 inflows and continued in every subsequent completed session shown, including Nov. 9 and 10.

The cohort score tracks weighted on-chain accumulation, while ETF flows measure net money entering or leaving those funds. Their units cannot be offset to determine whether large-wallet buying covered ETF withdrawals.

Neither dataset connects the accumulating groups to the other side of ETF redemptions, and identifying who acquired Bitcoin associated with those withdrawals would require information these measures don't provide.

A broader improvement would involve more wallet-size groups joining the Bitcoin accumulation signal, alongside sustained net ETF inflows. Those developments would strengthen the case that demand is spreading beyond the two accumulating groups.

For now, the two-cohort turn provides evidence of localized support, with the short-lived late-September accumulation showing why its persistence remains an open test.

#5121. New York federal judge denies Novig’s injunction bid, rules its sports event contracts are not swaps

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The decision allows the state to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or to prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

A federal judge in New York denied a preliminary injunction sought by sports prediction market platform Novig, ruling that its sports-related event contracts do not qualify as swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act.

According to a Tuesday court filing, U.S. District Judge Colleen McMahon of the Southern District of New York concluded that the state may apply its gambling and sports-wagering laws to Novig’s activity. The decision allows New York to require Novig either to obtain a state gambling license or prohibit the listing and trading of sports event contracts on its platform.

Ludlow Exchange, LLC, the legal entity behind Novig, received the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's designation as a contract market in June 2026 and began offering sports event contracts to New York customers in August.

On Aug. 5, 2026, the company sued New York Attorney General Letitia James and officials at the New York State Gaming Commission, arguing that federal commodities law preempts state regulation of its products. It contended that the CFTC has "exclusive jurisdiction" over event contracts, which qualify as swaps under the act.

Judge McMahon rejected that argument at the preliminary injunction stage. She wrote that Novig’s sports event contracts “bear more than a passing resemblance to sports betting” and fall outside the CEA’s definition of a swap.

"The Court concludes that Novig’s contracts do not depend on events or contingencies that are ‘associated with a potential financial, economic, or commercial consequence’ within the meaning of § 1a(47)(A)(ii). Accordingly, they are not ‘swaps’ under the CEA," the filing said.

The ruling follows a similar decision earlier this year by another New York federal judge that denied Kalshi's request to block state authorities from enforcing gambling laws on its prediction market platform. Novig is also litigating the same issue in multiple other states, as Kalshi, Polymarket, and other prediction market platforms are doing.

"NY Attorney General Letitia James could literally cross the street and file a state-court civil enforcement action against Novig as soon as today," sports betting and gaming lawyer Daniel Wallach wrote on X.

Wallach added that he's seeing a larger shift where state regulators may move from defending their enforcement authority to exercising it proactively in state court. The lawyer said federal courts within the Second Circuit have now issued 10 rulings in sports prediction-market litigation, all of which have favored the states.

The Block has reached out to Novig for comment.

#5118. 'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

'Tough Questions' Ahead: Warren Targets Prediction Markets, Trump Crypto as Banking Chair

Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), one of the crypto industry's most prominent critics, is preparing to use the chairmanship of the Senate Banking Committee to launch an oversight campaign aimed at Trump family crypto businesses, Big Tech and Wall Street, Politico reported on Monday.

"Big Tech CEOs, Administration officials, Trump family crypto businesses, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should preserve their documents and prepare to answer tough questions," a Warren Banking Committee aide told the outlet.

Warren is expected to take over the committee in January after Democrats won control of the Senate in last week's midterm elections. The panel oversees banking, housing, cryptocurrency and the Federal Reserve, and its chair has broad powers to call hearings and launch investigations.

According to the aide, Warren also wants to end a "Wild West-style lack of oversight" of prediction markets that she says has allowed insiders to profit, and plans to warn about threats to financial stability, including a potential AI bubble.

She intends to keep working with Republican China hawks to restrict exports of advanced AI chips, and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is expected to be a top oversight target, Politico reported. The report came a day after Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation into reports that Huang had been the target of a Chinese intelligence effort, sending Nvidia shares sliding as much as 8%.

Warren is also likely to investigate the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency's August decision to grant a conditional bank charter to Trump-backed World Liberty Financial, as well as the administration's firings of financial agency staff and its efforts to shut down the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, Reuters reported, citing a person familiar with her thinking. "You're going to see a lot of investigations," Brian Gardner, chief Washington policy strategist at Stifel, told the outlet.

In an essay for Time last week, Warren argued that Democrats won by "being populist economic fighters," and said "Big Tech CEOs, Wall Street executives, and giant corporations should prepare to answer tough questions and face meaningful consequences if they have broken the law."

Warren has been a persistent critic of the crypto industry and of the Trump family's business interests in it. She has argued that federal regulators' approval of national trust charters for crypto firms, including Coinbase and Ripple, is illegal, and has said the CFTC has been "steamrolled" by the prediction market and crypto firms it oversees.

She has also questioned Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over a Tether loan to a trust tied to his children, and joined Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) in urging the Labor Department to drop plans that would make it easier to hold crypto in 401(k) retirement plans.

Warren was one of the leading opponents of the Clarity Act, the crypto industry's top legislative priority, which stalled in the Senate this year. Democrats are now planning to push a broader crypto market structure bill when they take control of Congress.

The White House has pushed back. A spokesperson told Reuters that Trump is willing to work with anyone on behalf of Americans, but that "sham investigations and radical left policies" would undermine any such effort.

Republicans have cast Warren as the main obstacle to crypto legislation. Sen. Tim Scott (R-SC), the current Banking chair, has argued that without market structure rules, crypto is left in "the wild, wild West."

Warren's ability to pass legislation will be limited while Trump remains in the White House. In her Time essay, she acknowledged that Trump has embraced the nickname "Mr. Veto," but argued that Democrats should send him bills anyway.

Warren is also eyeing a more senior role in Senate Democratic leadership, potentially the No. 3 position, Politico reported last month.

#5115. Kraken parent Payward adds 200 tokenized UK and European equities to xStocks offering

The new listings are available on Kraken and other supported platforms, with Hong Kong equities to follow.

Kraken parent company Payward has launched more than 200 tokenized UK and European stocks through its xStocks framework, bringing the total offering to over 1,500 assets.

The first batch covers some of the most actively traded companies listed in the UK and European Union, according to a statement released Wednesday. Each token is backed 1:1 by shares held at a regulated custodian, with trading available through Kraken and supporting xStocks Alliance platforms. The tokens are also available across blockchain networks including Solana, Ethereum, TON, Ink, and X Layer.

Payward previously announced a partnership with the London Stock Exchange in September, with plans to bring the top 100 London-listed equities to xStocks. The LSE also plans to offer xStocks trading through LSE 24, pending regulatory approval.

"A portfolio should not have borders or trading hours," Payward Chief Commercial Officer Mark Greenberg said in Wednesday's release.

Payward partnered with GTN in July to expand xStocks beyond U.S. equities, initially outlining plans for Hong Kong, the UK, Europe, and South Korea. GTN provides the brokerage and custody infrastructure for the tokenized shares and the 200 UK and EU listings are the first to launch under that partnership. Payward said it expects to introduce "hundreds" of Hong Kong-listed stocks over the next few weeks, followed by listings from other international markets.

The xStocks service started in June 2025 with tokenized U.S. equities and exchange-traded funds. Payward put assets under management at over $1.1 billion on Wednesday and reported more than $45 billion in cumulative transaction volume and over 550,000 unique holders. Those figures compare with the $40 billion in volume and more than 200,000 holders previously reported by The Block in September.

Payward said xStocks are accessible in more than 110 markets, but UK investors still cannot access them. The offering is also unavailable in the U.S. or to U.S. persons and Payward said the latest additions would not change the existing rules on who can trade xStocks.

#5113. Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia and CEO Jensen Huang have been sued by a shareholder who accuses them of defrauding investors by concealing Huang's alleged relationship with a suspected Chinese spy.

The proposed class action, filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on Monday, accuses Nvidia and Huang of making materially false and misleading statements about the company's dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, according to the complaint. It alleges that Huang "had an affair with Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive and suspected Chinese spy," and that the defendants failed to disclose that this raised national security concerns posing a material risk to Nvidia's reputation and business prospects.

The plaintiff, Mark Kistenmacher, seeks to represent investors who bought Nvidia stock between October 9, 2025, and November 8, 2026. The suit accuses Nvidia and Huang of violating Section 10(b) of the Securities Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5, and Huang of violating Section 20(a), which can hold executives liable alongside a company. It seeks unspecified damages and a jury trial. Kistenmacher is represented by The Rosen Law Firm.

The complaint targets four statements. Two came from Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo in October 2025, when he told The New York Times that Nvidia's employees had "found no evidence of diversion" at Megaspeed and that Huang's visits to customer events were routine. A third came from Rizzo last month, when he said Huang's interactions with Alice Huang were "not unusual or unexpected." The fourth was Huang's own description of Nvidia's dealings with Megaspeed as "normal and not unusual" in an interview with Bloomberg on October 30. Each was misleading, the suit alleges, because of Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, is not related to Jensen Huang, Reuters reported. The New York Times reported on Sunday that Nvidia opened its investigation after Huang acknowledged to the company's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang. The newspaper also reported that U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio oversaw the drafting of a classified memo this spring warning top administration officials about the relationship, which was circulated ahead of U.S. President Donald Trump's trip to China in May.

Decrypt has reached out to Nvidia for comment. The allegations in the complaint have not been tested in court, and it is common for shareholders to sue companies after sharp share price declines.

The lawsuit was filed five minutes after U.S. markets closed on Monday, according to Reuters. Nvidia shares closed down 4.3% at $178.67 that day, according to Yahoo Finance, reducing its market value by about $190 billion, after having slid as much as 8% earlier in the session. The stock was flat on the day at $179.70 in premarket trading on Tuesday.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation on Sunday into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, saying it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. In an email to staff the same day, Huang apologized for being unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved.

The New York Times reported last month that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, and the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said Jensen Huang had been romantically involved with her. China's embassy in Washington has dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears."

Trump weighed in on Monday. "I asked him about that and he said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy," the president told reporters, according to The Guardian. He added that the FBI was "looking into it in terms of something else," without elaborating. A day earlier, Trump told reporters he did not regret his decision not to act on the intelligence, and that he believed Huang should stay on as CEO. "I think he's an honorable guy," he said, according to the Times.

#5109. Bitcoin Heated Hot Tubs and 278 Washington Bitcoin Merchants : Now Accepting Bitcoin

Washington Bitcoin merchants, from Seattle coffee shops to a Snorkel hot tub heated by bitcoin miners.

Washington Bitcoin merchant list has 278 businesses, from coffee shops and salons to a hot tub maker that heats its tubs with bitcoin mining. The list spans 94 named cities, and Seattle leads the way with 46 listings. First up is a homegrown US manufacturing business from Washington that makes a super unique product.

Snorkel has been making hot tubs in the Pacific Northwest since 1979, and its wood-stove business dates to the late 1970s. What makes them special is their full cedar tubs, and the wood stove which is fully submerged into the water of the cedar hot tub and has the “snorkel” coming out of the water. This is very convenient for anyone who has no electrical hook up.

It started with a bet in 2025. Michael Diederich, the owner, began accepting bitcoin for hot tubs after a wager with his brother. Payments were slow to trickle in at first, but bitcoiners kept asking him to build a hot tub powered by bitcoin miners. He started building one, pairing the tub with miners that heat the water. Snorkel works with Dane Sjoden of Hashrate House on the build.

The prototype with two S19J Pro miners drawing about 8 kW, which it says heated a tub from cold to 104°F (40°C) in under four hours. The project won an innovation award at the Heat Punk Summit in Denver, and a Snorkel tub is on display in Denver. Diederich sees a future where every electric hot tub is heated by miners, because the mining income helps pay back the cost of energy that would otherwise be wasted as heat.

The Rose sits at 226 Main Ave S in downtown Renton, in the Old Renton Pub building. Owner Katie Newman opened it in October 2020 as a gift shop that happened to serve coffee, and the coffee side grew into the larger part of the business. The shop stocks locally roasted coffee, handpicked artisan goods, and grab-and-go food. It won Best Customer Service in the 2025 Best of Renton readers’ poll.

Overcast Coffee is on Capitol Hill, and it started as a walk-up counter at 312 15th Ave E beside Kaiser Permanente. Owner Jonathan Pak opened the first shop there in June 2020. A second location, at 1517 12th Ave inside the Metier bike complex, came later, and the company buys its coffee and ingredients from small, local Seattle businesses.

#5108. Crosschain protocol Across restores systems after domain hijack

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Security platform GoPlus reported that total user losses are estimated at $350,000, with funds stolen from wallets on Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, BNB Chain, Ink, and Plasma.

Crosschain interoperability protocol Across Across Protocol (ACX) has restored control of its website and resumed normal operations on Wednesday after a domain takeover that redirected visitors to a phishing site.

At around 18:45 UTC on Nov. 10, attackers gained control of the protocol's official domain through a social engineering attack on its domain registrar, Namecheap, Across said in a post on X.

Across said it received an alert within 15 minutes of the takeover and began efforts to take the site offline. Security response group SEAL911 flagged it as a phishing domain with major wallet providers, while Cloudflare marked the domain at risk and subsequently removed its DNS records, rendering the phishing site inaccessible.

While Across did not disclose the number of impacted users or losses, security platform GoPlus reported that total user losses are estimated at $350,000, with funds stolen from wallets on Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, BNB Chain, Ink, and Plasma.

GoPlus detailed that the phishing page lured users into signing fake token approvals or permits designed to drain wallets.

Later on Wednesday, Across announced that it had regained control of the domain with assistance from Namecheap, and restored all systems to normal operations. It added that all affected users would be made whole.

Separately, decentralized exchange Arcus said on Tuesday that it lost around $2.23 million in USDG stablecoins due to an accounting bug that allowed an account to record a larger balance than it actually held. The exploiter requested another $3 million in withdrawals that were prevented by the rate limit on its bridge.

The DEX said no other user accounts were impacted, and it has fully covered the temporary shortfall with the company treasury.

Suiball, a Sui-native hardware wallet project, also announced closure on Tuesday, after the Sui Foundation decided that backing the project was too risky due to a recent wave of attacks on crypto hardware wallets.

These incidents follow a surge in security breaches and phishing campaigns accelerated by AI tools that enable faster and more sophisticated attacks. DefiLlama data shows $8.78 billion has been lost across 341 crypto hacks and exploits year-to-date, led by a $6.36 billion breach at Binance.

#5105. Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance has stopped processing transactions involving MEXC, one of the world's largest crypto exchanges, days after the U.S. Treasury Department designated MEXC a primary money laundering concern.

"Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC affiliates or related entities," Binance tweeted on Tuesday.

Binance is applying enhanced compliance measures following FinCEN’s designation of MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC…

Any request to send funds from a Binance account to MEXC will be declined, according to a Binance announcement that lists 13 entities covered by the restriction, including MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ and MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd. Deposits received from those entities will be temporarily withheld for compliance review, and users could face further restrictions, including being offboarded from Binance, the exchange said.

MEXC ranks ninth among spot exchanges on CoinMarketCap, which scores platforms on traffic, liquidity and trading volume, with about $812.5 million in spot volume over the past 24 hours. Binance ranks first, with about $6.4 billion.

The Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) named MEXC a primary money laundering concern last Friday, in connection with Russian illicit finance. The order, issued under the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing fund transfers involving the exchange.

FinCEN alleges that MEXC processed at least $16 billion in suspicious transactions between May 2021 and May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion in direct and indirect exposure to Russian sanctions evasion, and described its anti-money laundering controls as severely deficient.

The order takes effect 30 days after its publication in the Federal Register.

Binance is not the only exchange to respond. OKX has also warned its users against transferring funds to MEXC, Finance Magnates reported.

#5104. Deutsche Bank Switches On Bitcoin Custody for Paying Clients

Deutsche Bank has switched on its digital asset custody service for paying clients, the German lender said Wednesday, letting institutions and corporations in Europe hold bitcoin with the country’s largest bank less than two months after it first unveiled the plan.

The bank announced the service on Sept. 16, saying it would go live with first clients this year once regulators signed off. It now supports bitcoin and ether, along with the USDC, EURC and EURAU stablecoins, according to its product page.

Under the setup, Deutsche Bank manages the wallets and private keys on behalf of clients, who can store the assets and transfer them to third parties. The bank pitched that as a way for clients to skip building and maintaining their own custody infrastructure.

“In the medium term we aim to deliver a fully integrated, regulated ecosystem that supports the custody, tokenisation, and settlement of digital assets,” said Paul Maley, global head of trust and securities services at Deutsche Bank.

The bank did not name its first clients, say how many have signed up or disclose what it charges to hold the coins. The service is aimed at institutional and corporate clients, not retail customers.

The product page describes private keys stored in hardware security modules, a choice of warm and cold storage, multi-factor authentication, segregation of client assets and a customizable approval process for client transfers. Clients reach the service through Autobahn, Deutsche Bank’s multi-channel gateway, or its Corporate Bank portal.

The service is booked out of Deutsche Bank AG in Frankfurt and is available in Germany, with cross-border servicing across Europe through passporting. The bank lists institutional investors, multinationals and corporates, governments and sovereigns, and wealth managers as target clients, and it describes the offering as compliant with the European Union’s MiCA crypto rules.

For now the menu is narrow. Deutsche lists custody, transfers and internal moves between warm and cold storage as its initial services, with “further capabilities planned.” The September release said the asset list may grow with client demand and that tokenized financial instruments are on the roadmap.

It also carried a warning familiar to anyone in bitcoin: crypto assets held this way are not covered by a deposit-guarantee scheme like the one that protects eligible bank deposits.

Alongside the launch, Deutsche Bank signed a memorandum of understanding with Deutsche Asset Management, the money manager it mostly owns that was known until recently as DWS. The agreement is meant “to explore comprehensive commercial and technical collaboration opportunities in the digital asset space,” the bank said.

The firms plan to look at tokenized money market funds, crypto exchange-traded commodities and stablecoins built around the new custody platform, and Deutsche Asset Management’s chief product officer, Manfred Bauer, said it plans to launch a tokenized money market fund, according to a report. The asset manager oversaw about €1.22 trillion as of Sept. 30.

Deutsche Asset Management already backs AllUnity, the joint venture that issues EURAU, one of the three stablecoins the bank’s custody service supports.

Deutsche Bank has also told Reuters it wants to bring the service to Asia. In October, the bank’s corporate banking chief said the lender planned to expand the platform into the region next year.

The launch puts Deutsche Bank alongside a growing list of large banks that now hold bitcoin for clients. BNY Mellon became the first major U.S. bank to offer digital asset custody in 2022, and this year Standard Chartered moved to offer custody in Singapore while South Africa’s Absa became the first bank on the continent to custody bitcoin.

In the U.S., Coinbase this week won a full federal trust charter from the OCC, another sign that custody is becoming a regulated banking business on both sides of the Atlantic.

The timing is not ideal for new money. Bitcoin traded near $73,900 Wednesday morning, close to its lowest level since August after a hotter-than-expected U.S. inflation report on Tuesday.

#5101. Bitcoin and Blockchain Basics

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

How the First Cryptocurrency Works in Practice

Bitcoin is a decentralized digital currency that operates on a peer-to-peer network and is secured through Proof of Work. Its blockchain is a public ledger made up of blocks of transactions, with each block referencing the previous block’s hash to create a verifiable, tamper-evident history. Private keys allow users to authorize the spending of their Bitcoin. Wallet software manages these keys, constructs and signs transactions, and broadcasts them to the network, where nodes independently verify them.

Bitcoin and blockchain basics are the starting point for understanding the cryptocurrency industry. This Campus 101 subject explores where Bitcoin came from, how a blockchain records transactions, why decentralization matters, how mining secures the network, and how users control and send Bitcoin.

The lesson summaries below give a short overview of the full course content. Take the full interactive Bitcoin & Blockchain Basics 101 course to explore each lesson in more depth through guided explanations, examples, and activities.

Establishes Bitcoin and blockchain as the starting point for understanding mining, monetary policy, wallets, transactions, and the wider crypto industry.

Connects Bitcoin’s creation to the 2008 financial crisis, Satoshi Nakamoto’s whitepaper, decentralized money, and early contributors.

Defines Bitcoin as peer-to-peer digital money with a fixed supply, Proof of Work security, and transparent but pseudonymous transactions.

Uses a digital-notebook analogy to explain blocks, hashes, hash pointers, Merkle trees, tamper evidence, and verifiable ledgers.

Explains how miners perform Proof of Work, find valid hashes, add blocks, and earn block rewards and transaction fees.

Covers the 21 million supply cap, predictable issuance, block rewards, halvings, and Bitcoin’s scarcity model.

Introduces market capitalization, hash rate, and mining difficulty as different measures of market size and network activity.

Distinguishes backward-compatible soft forks from incompatible hard forks, using SegWit and Bitcoin Cash as examples.

Lesson 8 - Securing Communication and Information

Explains how private keys, public keys, and digital signatures establish control and authorize transactions.

Compares hardware and software wallets and explains addresses, private-key protection, and seed-phrase recovery.

Introduces UTXOs, inputs, outputs, change, fees, mempools, and the path from signing to confirmation.

Considers Bitcoin as peer-to-peer money, a store-of-value asset, a commodity-like asset, and the foundation of a wider decentralized economy.

Bitcoin launched during a turbulent economic period, when many people were losing trust in traditional financial markets. Cryptocurrency offered an alternative approach: a financial system designed to operate without central authorities, banks, or intermediaries.

Satoshi Nakamoto’s 2008 whitepaper, Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System, outlined the idea of a decentralized, trustless, censorship-resistant form of money. Satoshi’s identity remains unknown, but the network launched in 2009 and became the starting point for the broader cryptocurrency ecosystem.

Other important topics include early contributors and thought leaders, including educators, cryptographers, and early adopters who helped make Bitcoin understandable, secure, and culturally important.

Bitcoin is presented as the first cryptocurrency. It is a decentralized digital currency with a fixed supply of 21 million coins, a peer-to-peer network, Proof of Work security, and transparent but pseudonymous transactions.

The important beginner idea is not only that Bitcoin is digital money. It is that Bitcoin showed how a network could record value transfers without relying on a single company, government, or bank to update the ledger.

Decentralization means the distribution of power and control across a network, rather than relying on a single entity. In Bitcoin, thousands of independently operated nodes verify and store transaction data. This helps ensure that no single party can easily alter, censor, or control the network.

Instead of trusting one central database, the network relies on many participants to maintain and check the ledger.

A useful mental model is this: imagine a blockchain as a digital notebook. Each page of the notebook is a block. Each block contains information such as transaction data. Each block is assigned a unique code, called a hash, that identifies its contents.

Each block also points back to the previous block. That link is what creates the chain. If someone tries to change the contents of an already verified block, the hash changes, making tampering apparent to the rest of the network.

Within each block, Merkle trees combine transaction hashes into a compact commitment to the block’s contents. Rewriting confirmed history would require an attacker to redo the Proof of Work for the altered block and catch up with the honest network, which makes tampering increasingly difficult as more blocks are added.

Mining is introduced as the work that secures the ledger. In Proof of Work, network participants compete to solve computational puzzles. The first miner to find a valid solution earns the right to add a new block of transactions to the blockchain and receive compensation through newly issued Bitcoin and transaction fees.

Miners act as accountants competing to validate Bitcoin transactions. Mining rigs perform many calculations to find a nonce that creates a hash matching the difficulty set by the network. Other nodes then check whether the proposed block follows the rules before accepting it.

Bitcoin’s supply is capped at 21 million coins. New Bitcoin enters the network through mining rewards, and those rewards are reduced through events known as halvings. This creates a predictable supply schedule because no centralized party can simply change the issuance schedule on its own.

Several common metrics describe different parts of the system. Market capitalization combines price and circulating supply to estimate the market value of issued Bitcoin. Hash rate estimates the computational power contributing to mining, while mining difficulty adjusts the challenge of finding a valid block so issuance remains relatively consistent as hash rate changes.

The focus then moves from the network to the user. Bitcoin uses asymmetric cryptography, including private keys, public keys, and digital signatures, to prove ownership and authorize transactions without revealing the private key itself.

A private key is like a secret PIN, password, or physical key. Whoever controls the private key controls the wallet’s assets. A public key is derived from the private key and acts more like the lock or mailbox: it can be shared, but it does not reveal the private key.

A Bitcoin address is a public identifier derived from the public key. It is shorter and easier to share, and it is where someone can receive Bitcoin.

Software wallets run on connected devices and prioritize convenience, while hardware wallets keep signing keys in a dedicated device and reduce some forms of online exposure. Many wallets use a seed phrase as a recovery backup. Anyone who obtains the private key or seed phrase may be able to control the funds, so neither should be shared.

Bitcoin uses the UTXO model, which stands for Unspent Transaction Output. A paper-bill analogy makes it easier to understand: you must spend the whole output and receive change back if needed.

If Alice has one UTXO worth 2 BTC and wants to send 1 BTC to Bob, she spends the full 2 BTC output as the transaction input. The transaction creates one output to Bob and another output back to Alice as change. The original output is then marked as spent and cannot be used again.

Transaction fees help miners decide which transactions to include in blocks. If the network is busy and a fee is too low, the transaction may remain in the mempool, a temporary holding area for valid transactions that have not yet been included in a block.

Other important topics include the difference between soft forks and hard forks. A soft fork is a backward-compatible upgrade, meaning updated nodes can still interact with older nodes. A hard fork is not backward-compatible and can lead to separate chains if participants do not agree on the same rules.

The final point is that Bitcoin now plays several roles in the financial and technological landscape. It is often discussed as a store-of-value asset, a highly liquid digital asset, and the foundation that introduced a broader decentralized economy.

Bitcoin: The first cryptocurrency and a peer-to-peer digital monetary network.

Blockchain: A ledger made of linked blocks that record transaction data.

Node: A network participant that stores, checks, or relays Bitcoin data.

Proof of Work: A consensus mechanism where miners expend computational resources to add valid blocks.

Miner: A participant that competes to create new blocks and earn rewards and fees.

Hash: A unique code generated from data; changing the data changes the hash.

Private key: The secret credential that authorizes control of funds.

Public key: A public value derived from a private key and used in transaction verification.

Bitcoin address: A shareable identifier used to receive Bitcoin.

UTXO: An unspent transaction output that can be used as an input in a future transaction.

Mempool: A temporary area for valid transactions waiting to be included in a block.

After Bitcoin and Blockchain Basics, useful next topics include:

Bitcoin is a decentralized digital currency operated by a peer-to-peer network and secured by Proof of Work.

A blockchain is a ledger made of blocks that point back to previous blocks, creating a tamper-evident chain of transaction history.

Decentralization distributes control across many participants instead of relying on one central operator.

Miners compete to find a valid hash, add a new block, and receive rewards and transaction fees.

A wallet helps a user manage private keys, public addresses, and transaction signing.
