#5099. Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance has warned iPhone users about a vulnerability in Apple's iOS that it says could let malicious apps read files stored by other apps, putting crypto wallet seed phrases, private keys and login credentials at risk.

The flaw, dubbed "worse_query," affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2, the exchange tweeted on Tuesday. "This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance," it said.

A vulnerability (worse_query) affecting iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1, and 27.2 allows malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys, and login credentials at risk across the entire ecosystem. This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.…

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, such as sideloaded or manually signed app files, and to update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions. Anyone who suspects a breach should move their funds to a new wallet created on a clean device and reset all active login sessions, it said.

iPhone apps normally run inside a sandbox, which stops them from accessing other apps' private files. A researcher who goes by forcequitOS published worse_query on GitHub as a sandbox escape for iOS 26.0 through 26.7.2 and iOS 27.2, describing it as a proof of concept for developers that "isn't useful by itself." Among the locations it can reach is the folder where iOS apps store their data, according to the repository, which calls it the "direct successor" to an earlier exploit, bad_query. There have been no confirmed reports of crypto being stolen using the flaw.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 on Monday, a day after the proof of concept was published. Its security notes list fixes for nine vulnerabilities, including an authorization issue in App Intents through which "an app may be able to access sensitive user data," as well as several Bluetooth and iMessage flaws.

However, the notes do not mention worse_query or its researcher, and Apple has not publicly confirmed whether the update addresses the flaw Binance described. At least one security expert believes it does. 23pds, chief information security officer at blockchain security firm SlowMist, urged users to update to iOS 27.2.1, saying in a tweet that the update would block the worse_query sandbox escape, along with other threats.

It is not the first time attackers have targeted Apple devices. Earlier this year, Google warned of iOS malware that targeted crypto apps on unpatched devices, while in August, the Dutch national cyber agency said hackers were exploiting a flaw in Apple's macOS Screen Sharing feature to plant Monero miners on Macs.

#5097. ICE and OKX Set Nov. 23 Tokenized Stock Launch After 67 Companies Opt Out

OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) will open their tokenized stock venue on Nov. 23, the companies said Wednesday, offering more than 70 assets under a temporary SEC exemption even as 67 companies, including Apple, Tesla and Meta, have already objected.

The OKX announcement said more than 70 assets are expected to be listed, including securities from Better.com, BitGo, Circle, Coinbase, Robinhood, Securitize and Strategy (MSTR), as well as 59 exchange-traded funds from Direxion. Strategy will also list its preferred stocks, such as STRC.

Access at launch is limited to eligible U.S. customers of OKX Inc. who have activated a CeDeFi Wallet, passed identity checks and completed onboarding with the joint venture, which is called OKXICE and is owned equally by ICE and OKC USA Holding Inc., an OKX affiliate. Trading will run around the clock in the OKX app.

“The future of finance is onchain, and we have an opportunity to build it here in America, with the standards, safeguards and ambition that investors expect,” said former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, who co-chairs OKXICE with Trabue Bland.

The companies did not say how many of the more than 70 assets will trade on the first day, or how much liquidity the pools will have at the open. The venue is not a registered exchange or broker-dealer and is not approved by the SEC, the announcement said.

Trading will run through automated market maker pools built on Uniswap v4 and deployed on X Layer, the Ethereum layer-2 network OKX operates. Only approved wallets can trade or add liquidity.

Securitize will provide the tokenization infrastructure. Each token represents a security entitlement backed one-for-one by a U.S. share held by a registered broker-dealer, and holders keep the usual shareholder rights, including dividends, voting and corporate actions, the venue said in a Nov. 5 public notice required under the exemption.

Customers will hold the tokens in their own wallets. OKXICE will not hold the assets or the private keys. The tokens will be paired with payment stablecoins such as USDC and USDG. The venue does not operate an order book, does not extend credit and does not conduct primary offerings, the notice said.

The same Nov. 5 notice lists 67 issuers that had sent a written objection by that date, among them Apple, Amazon, Tesla, Meta, Netflix, Nvidia, Microsoft, JPMorgan, Goldman Sachs, Costco, Nike and Home Depot. The list has grown from 50 objectors in an Oct. 30 notice.

The objections stem from the SEC’s five-year “Innovation Exemption,” issued Sept. 17, which lets tokenized securities venues trade blockchain versions of U.S. stocks through automated market makers without registering as exchanges. When an unaffiliated third party tokenizes a company’s stock, the venue must notify the issuer, which then has 30 days to object in writing.

The SEC’s innovation exemption page lists OKXICE TSV as having submitted a notice of intent on Oct. 4.

The names the companies highlighted are mostly crypto-linked. Strategy, the largest public bitcoin treasury company, is listing both its common stock and its preferreds. “Tokenization on the OKXICE TSV is a natural next step toward making Digital Credit accessible to investors around the world, 24 hours a day, seven days a week,” Strategy’s president and chief executive, Phong Le, said in the announcement, referring to preferreds such as STRC. Le also called bitcoin “Digital Capital.”

The test now is whether a venue that has lost Apple, Tesla, Nvidia and most of the rest of the largest U.S. companies can still draw enough trading to matter when it opens in 12 days, and whether any more issuers object before then.

For context, a tokenized stock is a blockchain token backed one-for-one by a real share. Under the SEC’s Innovation Exemption, the venue can list these tokens without the issuing company’s consent, unless the company objects within 30 days.

#5093. Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Vitalik Buterin spent Sunday on X telling Ethereum holders to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start valuing it as a store of value. “We should not feel sad if fee burn is less than 10% of breakeven indefinitely,” he wrote. “The value of ETH comes from elsewhere.”

That elsewhere is stability, which he ties to decentralization, censorship resistance and security. It replaces the ultrasound money pitch, the idea that network usage burns supply faster than staking creates it. The burn has not kept up in years. Through the first nine months of 2026 it offset about 2% of issuance while supply grew 0.64%, mostly because Ethereum’s own rollup roadmap moved activity and fees off the mainnet.

Buterin’s argument is that this is fine, and that chasing the burn would be worse. Maximizing fee revenue means wanting congestion and expensive blockspace. He warned it would turn Ethereum into “a world-scale major corporation” at the cost of what makes it worth using. The burn, he added, was “never a true floor” and came mostly from internal crypto churn like lending and “silly NFT purchases.”

Supporters are already building out the new frame. Ethlabs co-founder Binji Pande calls ETH a future-proof store of value and argues it beats Bitcoin on quantum and AI risk, citing eleven years of trading history and an Ethereum Foundation that holds under 0.2% of supply. Julian Ma of Ethlabs makes the related case that Ethereum’s security culture positions it as a programmable store of value as AI pressures cryptography.

#5090. Russia’s Central Bank Clears Exchange to Open Bitcoin and Crypto Trading on Nov. 22

The Bank of Russia said Wednesday that exchanges may start organized trading in cryptocurrencies on Nov. 22, once the central bank has approved their trading rules, a step that lands just ahead of Moscow Exchange’s planned Dec. 1 bitcoin launch.

The ordinance, which amends the Bank of Russia’s rules for organized trading, was registered by the Justice Ministry on Nov. 3 and published Wednesday on the regulator’s site. It takes effect 10 days after publication, on Nov. 22.

Exchanges will have to register the brokers, trust managers and central counterparties that take part in crypto trades, keep registers of orders and deals, and monitor the activity. They must also calculate a current price, a weighted-average price, a market price and a closing price, and report trading activity to the central bank in the same way they already do for other assets.

The regulator was clear about the order of operations. Exchanges may open crypto markets from Nov. 22, it said, “but first they must agree with the regulator on the rules for conducting such trading.” It did not say whether any venue has already cleared that review.

A separate ordinance, published Nov. 6 and due to take effect Nov. 17, sets the menu for ordinary buyers. The Bank of Russia approved bitcoin, ether and Tether’s USDT for purchase by non-qualified investors, and said exchanges may admit those assets to public trading from that date.

Non-qualified investors can buy through a broker, a trust manager or a registered crypto exchange office, on an exchange or over the counter. Purchases through a single intermediary may not exceed 300,000 rubles a year, roughly $3,600 at the official exchange rate. Qualified investors can access all cryptoassets with no purchase cap.

Both groups still have to pass a knowledge test and read a risk disclosure before they can trade. The list is limited to the most liquid assets that meet the law’s tests for market capitalization, average daily volume and at least five years of pricing history on foreign exchanges.

Moscow Exchange, Russia’s largest trading venue, announced on Nov. 2 that it plans to offer bitcoin, ether and USDT trades from Dec. 1, initially as a pilot while participants connect. Trading would run on its existing stock-market systems from 6:50 a.m. to 11:50 p.m. Moscow time, with the National Clearing Centre clearing the deals and clients able to settle the same day or the next.

Bitcoin Magazine reported the Dec. 1 plan last week, including the U.S. sanctions that already cover the exchange and its clearing house. Boris Blokhin, a senior managing director at the exchange, said in early October that Moscow Exchange would not need additional licenses to start and would use a partner settlement digital depository. Wednesday’s notice still requires the central bank to sign off on the trading rules themselves. The exchange has not said publicly whether that approval is already in hand.

SPB Exchange, the St. Petersburg venue, has said it wants to start organized crypto trading by the end of the year and is building a settlement digital depository of its own, Russian newspaper Vedomosti reported in October.

The market sits on Federal Law No. 282-FZ, “On Digital Currencies and Digital Rights,” whose main provisions took effect on Sept. 1. The law licenses exchanges, brokers, managers, depositories and exchangers, recognizes crypto as property and opens a supervised channel for cross-border trade. It still bans using crypto to pay for goods and services inside Russia. Officials have spent the fall standing up the rest of the plumbing, including a self-regulatory body for crypto exchanges and digital depositories.

The test now is not whether Russia will have a legal exchange market for bitcoin, but when the first venue actually opens, and whether a 300,000-ruble retail cap is enough to pull traders off the offshore desks they already use.

#5088. Crypto fraudster jailed for eight years over $2.7 million theft plot directed from prison

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The plot was foiled after the would-be accomplices failed to gain entry to the victims’ home, while Barr’s phone later exposed his role.

A Scottish court sentenced Robert Barr to eight years' imprisonment for orchestrating an attempted £2 million ($2.7 million) cryptocurrency theft from his prison cell at HMP Edinburgh.

Barr was serving a 21-month sentence for cryptocurrency-related fraud when he organized the operation. The High Court in Glasgow sentenced him alongside Sean Favier, who received four years, and Iain Doyle, who was jailed for five years.

The trio pleaded guilty to involvement in serious organized crime prior to their sentencing, the court said in a statement on Tuesday.

According to the court's account, the plan relied on AnyDesk, software that allows users to access and control another computer remotely. Barr wanted his accomplices to get inside the victims' home and make the homeowner download the program, allowing him to control the couple's computer remotely from prison.

Once connected, Barr planned to enter a receiving cryptocurrency address and move funds from the victims' wallet into accounts under his control.

However, the plan didn't get that far. The court heard that Favier and Doyle arrived at a couple's home at about 11:30 p.m. on April 17, 2024, but the occupants spotted them on their doorbell camera and refused to open the door. The pair left without gaining entry.

"This was a crime which married 21st-century computer sophistication with old-fashioned housebreaking," Lord Cubie said when sentencing the men.

The court was told that investigators uncovered Barr's failed heist after seizing his phone on June 25, 2024 and finding messages detailing his role in and instructions to Favier and Doyle.

Addressing Barr's previous conviction on crypto-related fraud, the judge said his decision to organize another offence from prison was a "material aggravating factor."

"In terms of the sentence to be imposed if you had been found guilty after trial, I would have imposed a sentence of eight years and ten months," the judge said.

Barr faces a serious crime prevention hearing on Nov. 17.

#5085. Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

#5083. MSCI Rules Today on Cutting Strategy (MSTR) From Its Indexes After a Draft List Marks It Deleted

MSCI will say Wednesday evening whether Strategy stays in its global stock indexes, after its own potential list already marked the largest corporate bitcoin holder as Deleted.

The index provider publishes its November 2026 Index Review shortly after 5 p.m. ET. MSCI said on Oct. 15 that the review would carry the final list of current constituents hit by the new screen, with any deletions taking effect at the close of Nov. 30.

MSCI’s list of potentially impacted securities, published with the Oct. 15 decision and built on Sept. 30 financial data and Oct. 9 market values, already marks five companies as Deleted: Strategy (MSTR), uranium holder Yellow Cake, Japan’s Metaplanet, Iceland’s Skel Fjárfestingafélag and micro-cap Upexi.

Strategy is the only large-cap name in the group and by float-adjusted market value accounts for more than 90% of the five. The company held 850,364 bitcoin as of Nov. 8, acquired at an average price of $75,456 a coin.

The rule does not mention bitcoin. MSCI first checks whether a company’s operating assets exceed 50% of total assets. Issuers that fail face five financial ratios, and a company flagged on four or more of them is ineligible for the MSCI Global Investable Market Indexes.

Buffers limit turnover. A company outside the indexes cannot be added if it fails the screen in either of its two most recent annual filing periods, but a current constituent is removed only if it fails in each of them. MSCI said it may also use the most recent interim filing.

That two-filing test is the question hanging over Strategy. It adopted fair-value accounting for its bitcoin at the start of 2025, so its fiscal 2024 statements carried the coins at cost less impairment, and MSCI has not said how it treats that earlier year. A Deleted mark implies MSCI’s draft reading is that Strategy already failed both periods, but it has not published the inputs behind that placement.

Strategy has argued that it reports its bitcoin treasury as an operating segment and books related gains and losses in operating expenses.

The screen replaces an approach aimed squarely at crypto. MSCI first proposed in late 2025 to exclude companies whose digital assets made up 50% or more of total assets, then dropped that plan in January and opened a broader consultation in August. The new rule also ends the interim measures MSCI placed on those companies in October 2025, including a freeze on share-count increases and a deferral of additions.

JUST IN: MSCI decides this afternoon whether to keep Strategy and Metaplanet in its global indexes.JPMorgan estimates removal could trigger up to $2.8 billion in forced selling from passive funds 👀 pic.twitter.com/dp7R47U35W

Funds that track an MSCI index have to hold what the index holds, so a deletion would force them to sell by the Nov. 30 close. JPMorgan analysts estimated last November that an MSCI exclusion could drive about $2.8 billion of outflows from Strategy, and as much as $8.8 billion more if other index providers followed. That estimate was built on MSCI’s earlier digital-asset proposal, not the October screen, and MSCI itself has published no figure.

A removal would not delist Strategy’s stock, and nothing in the rules forces the company to sell bitcoin. The pressure would land on the shares, and through them on the premium Strategy uses to fund new purchases by selling stock. In an August letter to MSCI, the company said a deletion “would have no meaningful impact on Strategy’s business.”

Strategy’s own cushion is already thin. It bought 1,036 bitcoin for $81.9 million in the week to Nov. 8, a purchase that left its holdings below that average cost. Bitcoin traded near $73,900 Wednesday.

If Strategy fails only its latest filing rather than both, it would not be deleted this round and would instead go on a watchlist. The potential list already places 17 other constituents there, including SharpLink, TON Strategy and Solana Company.

Tokyo-listed Metaplanet faces the same Deleted mark, after it sold bitcoin last quarter to show investors it could tap its holdings for liquidity. The test now is whether Wednesday’s official list sticks with those Deleted marks, or whether MSCI pulls any of the five back onto the watchlist.

#5081. Bitcoin Core discloses three patched security flaws, including high-severity mining bug

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Thousands of Bitcoin nodes granted trusted permissions to incoming Tor connections under certain configurations, according to one of the advisories.

Bitcoin Core publicly disclosed three security vulnerabilities on Tuesday, including a high-severity flaw that could prevent affected mining nodes from creating valid block templates.

The earliest of the three was reported in April 2025, with the other two reported in September last year. Bitcoin Core, the software used by many Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) nodes, issued fixes for each of them in October 2025.

The high-severity CVE-2025-62861 affected Bitcoin Core versions 28.0 to 28.2 and 29.0 to 29.1. Niklas Goegge reported it on Sept. 26, 2025. Bitcoin Core's TRUC "sibling eviction" rules were still being applied to transactions returning to the mempool after a chain reorganization. The bug could evict a transaction without removing its descendants, leaving the mempool inconsistent. Miners affected by the issue had to restart their nodes before they could create valid block templates again.

Eugene Siegel reported the medium-severity CVE-2025-62859 issue in April 2025. The vulnerability meant a malicious peer could exploit witness malleation in compact blocks to delay block processing by up to 10 minutes. The advisory said the attack could be repeated indefinitely against nodes running in "-blocksonly" mode, or where the attacker controlled all three high-bandwidth peers.

Bitcoin Core 30.0 shipped with fixes for all three vulnerabilities on Oct. 10, 2025, followed by version 29.2 on Oct. 14, 2025. The last vulnerable Bitcoin Core version went end of life on Oct. 27 this year, according to the advisories.

In May, The Block reported on a separate high-severity memory safety vulnerability that developers had also patched before publicly disclosing it.

#5079. Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic has set a price range of $70 to $78 per share for its initial public offering, according to an updated filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Tuesday.

The AI company plans to sell 825 million shares of Class A common stock, 801.4 million of them new shares and 23.6 million from existing shareholders, on the Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," which it revealed when it made its IPO filing public last month. At the top of the range, the offering would raise about $64.4 billion, including the selling shareholders' stake. Underwriters have an option to buy a further 123.75 million shares.

Anthropic expects to price the offering on November 18 and begin trading on November 19, and is targeting a retail allocation of about 20%, well above the norm for an IPO, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the details.

Based on the roughly 13.09 billion shares the filing says will be outstanding after the offering, the range implies a market value of about $916 billion to $1.02 trillion. On a fully diluted basis, the company could be worth up to $1.405 trillion according to CNBC, up from the $965 billion valuation it announced in a funding round in May.

Alongside the IPO, seven of the companies that supply Anthropic's computing power have agreed to buy a combined $26 billion of Class N common stock, which has no voting rights, at the IPO price. At the midpoint of the range, Google and Nvidia would each buy about $10 billion, AMD about $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion each, and TSMC about $500 million, according to the filing. Those shares will not be subject to the usual post-IPO lock-up period.

Anthropic expects net proceeds of about $84.6 billion from the IPO and the private placement combined at the midpoint, which it plans to use for general corporate purposes, including investment in compute and infrastructure, according to the filing.

The company reported revenue of $33.2 billion for the first nine months of 2026, up from $2.5 billion a year earlier. It posted a net loss of $104 billion over the period, most of it from a $94.3 billion non-cash charge tied to the rising value of its convertible notes and other instruments; its operating loss was $1.26 billion. Anthropic has also committed to about $900.9 billion of spending, mostly on compute and infrastructure, of which about $70.7 billion is due within the next 12 months.

Anthropic will list as a public benefit corporation with an unusual governance structure. Its Long-Term Benefit Trust, a body of financially disinterested trustees, will elect a majority of its board, while a single share of Class F stock held by an entity owned by its seven co-founders will carry 50.1% of the company's voting power on most matters, according to the filing. As a result, Anthropic will be a "controlled company" under Nasdaq rules, and holders of its Class A shares will have limited influence over major decisions, the filing warns.

The range comes after a turbulent week for AI stocks. Some major investors told The Wall Street Journal last week that Anthropic's expected $1.5 trillion valuation would likely need to come down following a sell-off in AI stocks, with one suggesting a figure closer to $1 trillion. Anthropic had been eyeing a valuation of around $2 trillion as recently as September, according to an earlier draft filing.

The IPO's order book is already covered at the proposed range, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the matter.

#5076. OKXICE sets Nov. 23 launch for tokenized securities venue under SEC innovation exemption

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

On Wednesday, OKX and ICE announced it would launch its TSV on Nov. 23 as part of the SEC's five-year innovation exemption.

OKXICE, the joint venture between OKX and NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange, plans to launch its tokenized securities venue at the end of the month, bringing the Securities and Exchange Commission's "innovation exemption" to life.

On Wednesday, OKX and ICE announced it would launch its TSV on Nov. 23 as part of the SEC's five-year innovation exemption that debuted in September to bring capital markets in the U.S. on-chain and not have to register as more traditional exchanges.

"The future of finance is onchain, and we have an opportunity to build it here in America, with the standards, safeguards and ambition that investors expect,” said former New York Governor and Co-Chair of OKXICE Andrew Cuomo, in a statement.

The announcement of the launch comes amid dozens of issuers that say they have objected to tokenized versions of their stock trading on OKXICE. As part of the innovation exemption, issuers can decide that they don't want their stock to trade on certain venues. In the latest filing from Nov. 5, 67 companies had opted out, including Meta Platforms, Inc. The Home Depot, Netflix, Tesla, and Costco.

On Wednesday, ICE and OKX said it has confirmed 70 assets so far from issuers including Coinbase, Circle and Robinhood, as well as 59 exchange-traded funds. The OKXICE's TSV is available to customers in the U.S. who have a CeDeFi Wallet and have completed onboarding, the companies said.

A few reasons have since been reported for why companies objected to trading on OKXICE. A spokesperson for one of the companies that opted out, Applied Optoelectronics, told the Wall Street Journal the fiber-optic maker objected because it was unsure how tokenization would affect its stock price, liquidity and volatility, and also had concerns about whether investors may be confused about the company's involvement in the offering.

Another major exchange, Nasdaq is also working on tokenization and has partnered with crypto exchange Kraken on it. In late September, the exchange posted a Q&A on the SEC's innovation exemption. Nasdaq has taken a different view in that it has pushed back against the SEC's innovation exemption and has said it plans to "opt its own stock out of Tokenized Securities Venues," said Nasdaq Chief Legal and Regulatory Officer John Zecca in an op-ed published by Barron's on Tuesday.

Zecca said tokenization can be a positive for share issuers, but said a framework for tokenization should protect the rights attached to the underlying shares, uphold the principle that a tokenized stock remains a stock, and ensure issuers have a meaningful role in shaping how their shares are tokenized.

"Our greater concern, however, is that the Innovation Exemption allows tokenized securities to trade outside the government-regulated National Market System, which maintains a market data, reporting, and transparency framework that all stock trading must abide by," Zecca said, adding that fragmentation could then occur.

#5067. Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Jupiter, the largest trading platform on Solana, is rolling out four new products this week as it aims to expand from a place to trade tokens into an all-in-one home for onchain assets.

The company announced a new mission on Tuesday, "to make the world's assets universally accessible and useful," alongside the releases, which cover discovering, owning, earning on and managing assets.

Jupiter built its business by aggregating liquidity from across Solana so that each trade gets the best available price. It says that approach has processed more than $1.3 trillion in cumulative aggregator volume, and that its wider ecosystem now holds about $1.7 billion in total value locked, according to DeFiLlama data from early November. Its lending market has passed $1 billion in active loans, the company said.

"What Google did for information, Jupiter is doing for assets," said Kash Dhanda, Jupiter's chief operating officer. "We started with the trade because it was the hardest problem on Solana, and aggregation solved it. That taught us something: aggregation doesn't just work for trading, it works for everything."

Dhanda has framed the goal in even bigger terms. "I'm a big believer that the era of do-it-yourself DeFi is over," he said in a livestreamed interview with Incrypted on October 29, recalling having "12 tabs open and a spreadsheet" to manage positions across different venues. He said he hopes Jupiter "in five years' time will be the only money app that you need to use."

Baskets let users buy a bundle of tokens with a single SOL or USDC deposit instead of making a separate swap for each one. Baskets can follow market-cap weights or fixed targets and rebalance automatically, with every deposit, rebalance and exit routed through Jupiter for the best available price.

Jupiter Vaults is a new hub for yield strategies, from stablecoin lending to strategies backed by real-world assets, managed by independent curators rather than Jupiter itself. Users can review each strategy's risk profile, allocation and historical returns before depositing. Vaults opens on November 11 as a private beta for more than 19,000 people on its waitlist and eligible holders of staked JUP.

In the October interview, Dhanda said Jupiter's yield products are audited multiple times before reaching users. Asked what would happen if a product were exploited, he said that "especially if it's our fault, we'd like to work with users to find a good solution," and that Jupiter is exploring ways to insure such products onchain.

Portfolio V3, live now, turns Jupiter's portfolio page into a place to act on positions, with quick actions to trade, open perpetual futures, make predictions or lend, and alerts for positions near liquidation. It also adds cross-chain transfers that combine several bridges.

Pulse and rebuilt token pages are meant to make Jupiter a place to research assets as well as trade them, showing market data for tokenized stocks and ETFs, tokenomics for DeFi projects, and a sidebar for comparing tokens. Dhanda has argued that putting assets onchain is only half the job. "People talk about tokenization as if it is the end game, when in reality, it is just the first step of the game," he said in October.

The releases will roll out over the coming days, with Jupiter sharing details of each one before Solana Breakpoint, the network's annual conference, which starts on November 15.

All Vaults strategies carry risk, including the possible loss of funds, and some may not be available in all regions, including the United States, Jupiter said.

Jupiter's launches come amid a flurry of activity across the Solana ecosystem ahead of the conference. Last week, Solana Labs' new company Leap launched a beta mobile app for trading tokenized stocks, ETFs, metals, currencies and crypto in one place.

The network has also been courting institutions. Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, while Polygon Labs extended its Open Money Stack, a suite of stablecoin payment tools linking bank accounts, wallets and payouts, to Solana.

Under the hood, Solana completed its biggest consensus upgrade since its 2020 launch in October, when Alpenglow went live and cut the time for a transaction to become irreversible from about 12.8 seconds to roughly 150 milliseconds. Solana treasury firm DeFi Development Corp has also added $3 million worth of SOL to its holdings.

Jupiter powers token swaps on The Information Exchange, Decrypt's trading platform.

#5063. Bitcoin ‘ripe for a squeeze’ as options traders raise odds of sub-$70,000 Christmas

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Derive's Christmas bitcoin options pricing has turned increasingly bearish over the past week, while K33 said the cryptocurrency is set for a squeeze in either direction.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) was trading around $73,500 on Wednesday after recording five consecutive weekly losses, with options traders now pricing a greater chance of the largest cryptocurrency dropping below $70,000 by Christmas than climbing above $80,000.

Last Wednesday, the platform put the odds of an $80,000-plus finish at 53%. That has now fallen to 26%, according to Derive founder and CEO Nick Forster, while the chance of a sub-$70,000 Christmas finish has risen from 15% to 31%.

Forster told The Block that traders are becoming increasingly defensive, with downside protection (puts) costing more than equivalent upside exposure (calls). Bitcoin options trading on Derive totaled $418 million last week, a 37% increase, and puts accounted for 55% of premiums traded compared to the previous week's share of 36%, he said.

"One of the most interesting trades came just hours before Thursday's tech sell-off, when a wallet spent approximately $14K protecting 300 BTC against a fall below $60K by November 27," Forster said. "The position could pay up to $3 million if bitcoin falls below $50K."

Bitcoin dropped back below $74,000 on Tuesday following the release of October's U.S. consumer price index. Prices increased 0.4% during the month against expectations of 0.3%, and futures traders priced in further Fed tightening at the December meeting. That followed more than $1 billion in crypto long liquidations on Monday as bitcoin fell below its 50-week moving average, with Friday's U.S. producer price index release next on the economic calendar.

Meanwhile, perpetual futures open interest stood at around 353,000 BTC on Tuesday, according to K33's latest research report, only higher on 1.2% of trading days over the previous two years.

Over the past 30 days, open interest increased 12%, and bitcoin lost 10%, K33 Head of Research Vetle Lunde noted, with open interest climbing to levels last seen near the October 2025 peak, towards the end of the November 2025 selloff, and ahead of the August rally.

"A breakdown could trigger substantial long liquidations, while a recovery could fuel short covering, amplifying price moves as leveraged positions unwind," Lunde said, adding that the market was "ripe for a squeeze."

K33 also noted a 0.75 correlation between bitcoin and the Nasdaq over 30 days, a reading in the 99th percentile since 2020. Reviewing 16 previous strong Nasdaq rebounds from market corrections, of the nine that continued higher, bitcoin gained in eight, Lunde said, with a median return of 2.9% over 20 days. Bitcoin fell in each of the seven failed rebounds, with a median loss of 21% in the following 20 days.

Meanwhile, U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs recorded net outflows of $1.28 billion last week, with a further $296 million exiting the ETFs on Monday and Tuesday, according to data compiled by The Block, as the funds also turned negative for the year. Global bitcoin ETPs have recorded withdrawals of 31,652 BTC over the past seven trading days, according to K33.

#5062. Bitcoin Falls as Hot US Inflation Fuels Fears of Bigger Fed Rate Hike

Bitcoin Falls as Hot US Inflation Fuels Fears of Bigger Fed Rate Hike

Bitcoin fell below $74,000 on Tuesday after U.S. inflation came in hotter than expected for the third consecutive month, adding fresh pressure to a crypto market already struggling with falling prices and investor withdrawals.

The Consumer Price Index rose 0.4% in October, above economists' forecasts of 0.3%, according to data released by the Bureau of Labor Statistics. Core CPI, which strips out food and energy prices, climbed 0.3% against expectations of 0.2%, matching September's reading for the third consecutive month.

Headline inflation actually slowed from September's 0.6% increase, but that offered little relief to traders who had expected a bigger slowdown. Both figures exceeded forecasts by 0.1 percentage points, giving the Federal Reserve another reason to keep interest rates elevated.

Bitcoin was trading near $74,200 before the announcement and briefly dropped to approximately $73,525 immediately afterward. The cryptocurrency recovered some ground toward $74,000 but remained under pressure, trading roughly 1% lower over 24 hours.

Ethereum also weakened, slipping toward $2,040 during the initial selloff, while other major altcoins faced selling pressure. Approximately $178 million in crypto long positions were liquidated within an hour of the announcement.

Liquidations happen when exchanges automatically close trades made with borrowed money because their owners no longer have enough collateral to cover potential losses. Those forced sales can push prices even lower, triggering further liquidations.

Bitcoin was already having a difficult week. The asset had lost roughly 4.5% over seven days, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded another $201 million in net withdrawals on Monday, extending the selling pressure that preceded the inflation report.

Higher inflation usually means higher interest rates, and higher interest rates tend to be bad news for crypto. When government bonds and cash offer attractive returns, investors have less incentive to hold volatile assets such as Bitcoin, which generate no interest simply by being held.

Higher borrowing costs also reduce the availability of cheap money for speculative investments. That's why Bitcoin, despite its reputation as an inflation hedge, frequently falls when inflation data suggests the Fed will tighten monetary policy.

The latest report landed just hours after Fed Governor Christopher Waller made unusually aggressive comments about the central bank's next move.

Speaking in London, Waller said he would support a quarter-percentage-point interest rate increase in December even if inflation improved. But he also warned that another core CPI reading of 0.3% could justify a larger move.

“I believe a 50 basis point hike in the policy rate should be considered at the December meeting,” he said.

October's reading hit precisely that threshold. According to Reuters, financial analysts increased their estimated probability of a 50-basis-point hike to nearly 15% by midmorning, although a smaller quarter-point increase remained the overwhelming favorite.

For context, the Fed currently holds its benchmark interest rate between 3.75% and 4%. A 50-basis-point increase would lift that range to 4.25%-4.50%, making borrowing more expensive across all of the economy.

Bond markets reacted accordingly. The two-year Treasury yield, which closely tracks expectations for Fed policy, posted its biggest increase since April 2025, while the dollar strengthened and U.S. stocks opened lower.

October's inflation report offered a mixed picture of the American economy. Food prices rose 0.5%, energy increased 0.7%, and shelter costs climbed 0.3%, accounting for more than 30% of the monthly headline increase.

Energy inflation slowed considerably from September's 5.2% surge, but price increases remained widespread across services. Recreation costs climbed 0.8%, motor vehicle insurance rose 0.7%, and medical care increased 0.3%.

The report also came with an unusual limitation: The government did not publish its standard annual inflation rate because the 2025 federal shutdown prevented the collection of October data that year. September's last available annual headline reading stood at 3.7%, well above the Fed's 2% inflation target, which is formally measured using a separate index known as Personal Consumption Expenditures.

Crypto investors have seen this pattern before. In October, Bitcoin fell after September's hotter-than-expected inflation report, and the selloff continued as rate-hike expectations strengthened.

This time, the market enters the next Fed meeting with Bitcoin substantially below its October levels and institutional demand showing signs of weakness. Higher Treasury yields could make a sustained recovery more difficult unless inflation cools or investors regain their appetite for risk.

The Fed's final policy meeting of 2026 is scheduled for December 8-9. The next CPI report will arrive on December 10, meaning policymakers will have to make their December rate decision without seeing November's consumer inflation figures.

#5058. StoneX sees 111% upside for Securitize shares as tokenization revenue rebounds

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

StoneX sees tokenized equities as a major growth opportunity, citing Securitize's regulatory registrations and advantages over venues facing issuer opt-outs.

StoneX Financial reiterated its Buy rating and $18 price target on Securitize, citing new revenue projections and growth opportunities in institutional fund distribution and tokenized equities.

Securitize shares closed at $8.53 on Tuesday, up 22.4% on the day, according to The Block's SECZ price page. StoneX's target implies roughly 111% upside from that level.

In a note to clients on Wednesday, analyst Mark Palmer said Securitize's third-quarter revenue of $18.6 million, record average tokenized assets of $5 billion, and 46% sequential rebound in its tokenization segment supported the firm's investment thesis.

StoneX expects revenue to reach $72.4 million in 2026, $108.2 million in 2027, and $167.9 million in 2028, with this year's target lying within the company's annual revenue guidance of $70 million to $80 million.

The forecasts assume average tokenized assets on the platform will approach $12 billion by 2028, driven by new fund launches, the expansion of Securitize Stocks, and distribution agreements in South Korea ahead of the country's first tokenization rules in February, Palmer said.

Palmer identified tokenized equities as a potentially larger opportunity for Securitize. Global equities represent a $169 trillion market, but only about 0.002% of that value is onchain, he said, adding that the tokenized stock market grew fivefold over the past year to $3.2 billion.

Palmer cited CEO Carlos Domingo's comments that Securitize operates under permanent broker-dealer, alternative trading system, and transfer agent registrations, meaning its tokenized stock entitlements do not require issuer consent. That differs from venues operating under the SEC's temporary Innovation Exemption, where some issuers have opted out of tokenized trading pilots.

Securitize launched its tokenized stock product last month, giving eligible investors exposure to U.S. equities through digital tokens backed one-for-one by the underlying shares. The platform had listed 25 stocks by Nov. 2.

Stonex said the company also earns a share of management fees as transfer agent and tokenization provider for BlackRock's BRSRV money market fund. Its Securitize Capital subsidiary collects fees from the Neuberger Berman high-income fund and shares them with the manager as sub-adviser.

"The company’s tokenized assets are at a record level, the world’s largest asset managers continue to choose its platform for their onchain products, and it has the capital, licenses, and product set to pursue the whitespace in tokenized equities and credit," Palmer wrote.

Securitize also recently agreed to acquire River One, a structured-credit and asset-backed finance platform incubated by Framework Ventures and backed by Maple Finance. CEO Carlos Domingo said the acquisition would allow Securitize to expand into loan origination, generating origination fees and a share of net interest margin alongside its existing servicing and distribution revenue.

#5056. Goldman, Citi and Global Banks Commit $270 Million to Challenge Crypto’s Stablecoin Giants

Goldman, Citi and Global Banks Commit $270 Million to Challenge Crypto’s Stablecoin Giants

The group of global banks building a joint stablecoin has put real money on the table, committing around $270 million to fund the venture as it moves from blueprint to buildout.

Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, Banco Santander, and Deutsche Bank are among the institutions backing the effort, Bloomberg reported Tuesday, citing people familiar with the matter.

The capital is meant to cover five years of operations for a planned U.S.-incorporated company that will issue a dollar-denominated stablecoin. Known among participants as Project Faro, the venture is still searching for a chief executive, and its final name remains under discussion.

A spokesperson for the project declined to comment, as did Santander and Deutsche Bank, while Goldman and Citi referred questions to the project.

The funding is the concrete next step for an initiative that first went public late last year. As Decrypt previously reported, 21 of the world’s largest financial institutions committed in September to form a company to issue a U.S. dollar stablecoin, targeting a launch in the first half of 2027, with a euro version and other currencies to follow.

That consortium had more than doubled from an initial group of 10 banks that began exploring the idea in October 2025, and now spans North America, Europe, East Asia, the Middle East and Africa.

Beyond the four named by Bloomberg, its participants include Bank of America, Wells Fargo, Fidelity Investments, PNC, Capital One, TD Bank, UBS, BBVA, Deutsche Bank, Lloyds Banking Group and MUFG, among others. JPMorgan, notably, is not on the list.

The venture is one of several collaborative pushes by established finance into a business long dominated by crypto-native players. Visa, Mastercard and Stripe are among more than 100 companies backing the issuer Open Standard, while European banks have formed Qivalis to issue a euro-denominated coin.

At the moment, Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC together account for about 85% of a market worth roughly $290 billion, nearly all of it dollar-pegged. When the bank consortium first went public in September, Circle’s stock fell about 6% as investors priced in the prospect of bank-backed competition for USDC.

The banks are betting that institutional trust, regulatory alignment and existing client networks can win them a share of a settlement business that promises round-the-clock, cross-border payments at lower cost than traditional rails.

The token is being designed to comply with the U.S. GENIUS Act, which created the first federal framework for dollar-pegged tokens in 2025, and, where applicable, the EU’s MiCA regime. The group has said it wants the stablecoin used across wholesale, institutional and retail markets.

For now, Project Faro remains pre-launch, with no issued token, no named CEO, not even a final name, and a 2027 target that leaves ample room for delay.

But the $270 million commitment marks a shift, and a concrete signal that this bank consortium intends to compete for the onchain dollar rather than leave it to crypto.

#5055. 5 Best Acupressure Pens for Targeted Massage, According to Experts

Here’s how to use one, and what to expert.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

There's a lot coming at you on a daily basis to stress you out. But even though you probably lean into the big de-stressors like exercising regularly, doing breathing exercises on demand, and talk therapy as needed, sometimes you just need a way to dial down the tension building up inside you without visiting a provider's office or hitting the gym. That's where acupressure pens come in.

These handheld tools, which are sometimes called acupuncture pens, are designed to target pressure points in your body to help relieve tension and pain. "Acupressure stimulates acupuncture points without using needles," says Tom Ingegno, DACM, acupuncturist and founder of Charm City Integrative Health. "It lets people take a more active role in their own health without necessarily seeing a licensed [acupuncture] practitioner."

An acupressure pen has a shape that's optimized to stimulate acupuncture points, or areas on the body that are linked to certain health benefits, explains Margie Navarro, DAOM, doctor of acupuncture at Vivacity Healthcare in New York City. "The tool has a rather small head that is smooth and rounded, so that it is a relatively comfortable way to stimulate pressure on a small area or point," she says.

People use these tools to ease anxiety, headaches, and general pain, and there is some data to suggest acupressure in general may be helpful in treating all of these. (Because each acupressure pen is slightly different, it's a little tricky to prove that they'll work for these issues.)

Still, acupuncture experts say these pens are unlikely to hurt if you want to give them a try. "It gives people a way to play an active role in their health," Navarro says. "Even just a sense of agency can improve health outcomes." Sounds like something you can get behind? These acupressure pens can get you started.

There are a ton of acupressure pens out there to choose from, and most can be used all over your body. Odacite's Mon Ami tool was specifically designed with facial acupressure in mind, with a rounded tip on one end and a spatula-scoop hybrid on the other. That allows users to apply products like moisturizers and serums to targeted areas, before massaging it in.

This metal pen feels cool to the touch, creating a soothing feel as you work out muscle tension. It also comes with a pretty pink carry bag to make it easy to tote it with you while you travel. Just be aware that you'll need to clean this regularly, especially if you use it with beauty products.

Most acupressure pens are inexpensive, but the Wellgler pen is a mere $7, making it the cheapest option on our list. The acupressure pen has two different rounded heads, allowing you to really get in there for smaller areas you want to target, like TMJ muscles. But the larger end is also a good option for massaging the rest of your face, and even your sinuses.

This pen feels a little weightier than you'd expect for $7—that can be a pro or a con, depending on your personal preference in an acupressure pen. It also has contoured grips on both ends, so you can get a comfortable, secure grasp while massaging or pressing on any given area.

The big draw of an acupressure pen is its tips—they're what you use to target pressure points, after all. But the Feelfree acupressure pen has a long, tapered tip that you can use to roll over various parts of your body, giving you even more options than "just" acupressure.

The dual rounded ends feature different sizes, so you can target your TMJ muscles with one end, before easing tension in your neck with the other, larger end. It comes with a microfiber cloth for easy cleaning, along with a travel bag, so you can easily do acupressure on the go.

Most acupressure pens you'll find are made of some kind of metal, both for a cooling feel against your skin and for ease of cleaning. The Yutiann acupressure pen is made of wood for a gentler feel. This acupressure pen is relatively large, at 6.1 centimeters, giving you more options for use.

You can use the pen and its length on your hands and face, along with your back and feet. Multiple grooves near the top of the pen create a molded feel for a comfortable grip that you may not get as easily with smaller metal pens. Just be aware that wooden tools aren't as easy to clean as their metal counterparts. This pen's larger size also means the smaller tip may not be petite enough to target teeny tense muscles.

This Rena Chris set gives you a little of everything for working out stress and pain in your face. It features a contoured acupressure pen, along with a gua sha tool, both made of a sturdy stainless steel. So, you can do targeted acupressure, followed by gua sha to ease tension and shuttle away swelling.

This set has a reflexology guide to show various pressure points on your hands, feet, and ears that you may want to touch to soothe whatever ails you. It also comes with a cleaning cloth to make clean-up a breeze. Just be aware that there's no included travel bag, making you on your own for that one.

You have a few options for use with an acupressure pen. In general, these are designed to stimulate pressure points in your body. Most people use acupressure pens for some type of pain relief, whether it's headaches or TMJ discomfort. But these pens can also be used to ease anxiety and stress.

Many acupressure pens are made of medical-grade stainless steel. However, some are made from brass or other metals. You can also find acupressure pens made of wood, although these are less common.

Every pen is slightly different, making it important to read the manufacturer's directions first. But you can typically clean an acupressure pen by wiping it with a soft cloth before you use it.

Acupressure pens shouldn't break your budget, and most cost $25 or less. "The key is the stimulation of the point, not necessarily the tool you're using," Ingegno says. "You don't need an expensive device to practice acupressure, but a pen can make it easier."

There is data to suggest that acupressure can help with easing a range of pain and discomfort. "Manual pressure can have therapeutic benefits in a variety of conditions, including muscular pain," says Navarro. But you need to know which areas to target. And, of course, not everyone will feel pain or stress relief from acupressure, regardless of whether you get it from a pen or a provider.

Ingegno suggests learning about pressure points in the body before using an acupressure pen. "I used to recommend books, but these days there's so much information online that it's pretty easy to find a starting point," he says. Once you've figured out the area you want to target, he suggests applying gentle to moderate pressure for about 30 to 60 seconds. "Some points may feel a little sore or tender when pressed, and that's not necessarily a bad thing, but you shouldn't be causing yourself pain," Ingegno says. "More pressure isn't always better."

You can apply steady pressure on a point or gently rub or massage the area with the tool, according to Ingegno. "Neither approach is wrong, and both can be useful," he says.

There are electronic acupressure pens, which deliver small electrical impulses to pressure points. "Follow the manufacturer's instructions, since there are several different types available and they don't all work the same way," Ingegno says.

Most people can safely use acupressure pens, according to Ingegno. But there are a few caveats. If you're pregnant, he recommends taking a pass. If you're using blood thinners, Ingegno also suggests being gentle, since you may bruise more easily. "Electronic acupressure pens also require a few additional precautions," Ingegno says. "People with pacemakers or other implanted electronic medical devices should avoid electrical stimulation unless their healthcare provider has specifically cleared them."

If you have broken skin or areas where the tissue is already damaged, Navarro recommends avoiding using the acupressure pen on that area.

#5052. Pyth Network adds NYSE stock and ETF data to its marketplace

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The addition follows Pyth's Nasdaq data deal, allowing apps to access data from both exchange groups through the same marketplace.

Pyth Network, a blockchain oracle network that provides market data to financial apps, has added real-time stock and ETF data from the New York Stock Exchange to its marketplace.

Pyth can now distribute NYSE BQT, which provides stock quotes and trade data from all five NYSE Group equity exchanges, the network said Wednesday. In September, Pyth also received approval to distribute Nasdaq Basic, now allowing apps to access data from both exchange groups through the same marketplace.

Clients must first get a license from NYSE to access the data through Pyth, the network said, noting that NYSE's data rules and agreements will continue to apply.

The data includes best bid and offer with size, trade prices, official closing prices, total trading volume, and alerts when trading is stopped or restarted, or short-sale restrictions apply. The feed covers NYSE, NYSE American, NYSE Arca, NYSE National and NYSE Texas. It also includes stocks and ETFs listed on other U.S. exchanges if they are traded on NYSE Group exchanges, Pyth said.

"As financial markets increasingly move onchain, the quality and integrity of the underlying data matter more than ever," Anthony Zawadzki, head of data and digital asset products at NYSE, said in a statement. He added that the collaboration gives builders and institutions another way to access NYSE data.

Pyth said platforms offering stock perpetual futures or tokenized shares can use the NYSE data to show users stock prices. NYSE provides data during market hours, while Pyth's own indices provide prices when markets are closed.

"Pyth Indices price U.S. equities 24/7. NYSE BQT carries the primary market close. Together, an app can show a user exactly how far a stock has moved since NYSE closed, through the night and over the weekend," the network said.

Pyth launched its data marketplace in April, with data providers including Euronext FX, Fidelity Investments, OTC Markets Group, SGX FX, Tradeweb and Exchange Data International.

The marketplace allows institutions to provide their data to other companies while deciding how much to charge, how their data is credited, and the terms for using it, according to Pyth.

#5050. Grayscale Bets $1 Million on Zcash as Privacy Coin Tumbles

#5049. MSCI Removes Strategy (MSTR) From Its USA Index, Forcing Index Funds to Sell by Nov. 30

MSCI will remove Strategy, the largest corporate bitcoin holder, from the MSCI USA Index as of the close of Nov. 30, the index provider said Wednesday, after a new screen classified the company as a non-operating issuer.

The decision is part of MSCI’s November 2026 Index Review, posted shortly after 5 p.m. ET. Official additions and deletions lists show Strategy (MSTR) out of its USA Index, which also takes it out of the broader MSCI World and ACWI indexes. The same review deletes Japan’s Metaplanet from the MSCI Japan Small Cap Index and uranium holder Yellow Cake from the MSCI United Kingdom Small Cap Index, and it removes Iceland’s Skel Fjárfestingafélag from the MSCI Iceland Frontier Markets Small Cap Index and micro-cap Upexi from the USA Micro Cap Index. All five had already been marked Deleted on MSCI’s potential-impact list, built on Sept. 30 financial data and Oct. 9 market values.

Strategy is the only large-cap name in the group and, on that October list, accounted for more than 90% of the five companies’ combined float-adjusted market value. The company held 850,364 bitcoin as of Nov. 8, acquired at an average price of $75,456 a coin, or $64.16 billion in aggregate.

Strategy shares closed at $122.35, up 1.06%, then slipped 1.51% to $120.50 in after-hours trading as of 5:34 p.m. ET.

The rule does not mention bitcoin. The index first checks whether a company’s operating assets equal at least 50% of total assets. Issuers that fail that core screen face five financial ratios, and a company flagged on four or more of them is ineligible for the MSCI Global Investable Market Indexes.

Buffers limit turnover. A company outside the indexes cannot be added if it fails the ineligibility screen in either of its two most recent annual filing periods, but a current constituent is removed only if it fails in each of them. The index said it may also use the most recent interim filing.

MSCI also said Wednesday that the same non-operating treatment will apply to the MSCI US Index Series.

The screen replaces an approach aimed squarely at crypto. The index first proposed in late 2025 to exclude companies whose digital assets made up 50% or more of total assets, then dropped that plan in January and opened a broader consultation in August. It adopted the new methodology on Oct. 15, with minor modifications, and said it would end the interim measures it had placed on those companies in October 2025, including a freeze on share-count increases and a deferral of additions.

Funds that track an MSCI index have to hold what the index holds, so they will have to sell the deleted stocks by the Nov. 30 close. JPMorgan analysts estimated last November that an exclusion could drive about $2.8 billion of outflows from Strategy, and as much as $8.8 billion more if other index providers followed. That estimate was built on MSCI’s earlier digital-asset proposal, not the October screen, and MSCI itself has published no figure.

A removal does not delist Strategy’s stock, and nothing in the rules forces the company to sell bitcoin. The pressure lands on the shares, and through them on the premium Strategy uses to fund new purchases by selling stock.

In an August letter, the company said a deletion “would have no meaningful impact on Strategy’s business.” Strategy has also said funds tracking MSCI’s global investable indexes hold 3.1% of its basic shares outstanding, and that it reports its bitcoin treasury as an operating segment under U.S. GAAP.

Seventeen other constituents were on the potential watchlist, including SharpLink, TON Strategy and Solana Company. Those names do not appear among Wednesday’s published deletions.

Tokyo-listed Metaplanet is the other bitcoin treasury firm in the deleted group, after it sold bitcoin last quarter to show investors it could tap its holdings for liquidity. Strategy bought 1,036 bitcoin for $81.9 million in the week to Nov. 8, a purchase that left its holdings below that average cost.

#5046. Your Guide to the 2026 NWSL Playoffs: Teams, Schedule, and How to Watch

After a long season, the stage is set for the National Women’s Soccer League playoffs. Eight teams will compete to see who will be crowned the 2026 champions at the end of the month. And there's been plenty of impressive soccer leading up to this point.

This year, the Washington Spirit's Ashley Sanchez tied the single-season league record with 20 goals to get the Golden Boot award. (Croix Bethune of the Kansas City Current set a league record for the most assists, too.)

Angel City FC went into the postseason with an impressive 10-match unbeaten streak, making them a big team to watch. The Seattle Reign also locked down the 8th spot in the playoffs in dramatic fashion with a 1-0 win over the Orlando Pride.

There were also big changes made to future player contracts, with the NWSL dropping the so-called "Rodman rule" that allows any player in the league to earn the NWSL's highest possible salaries. That goes into effect on January 1.

Finally, the regular season wrapped up with Decision Day on November 1, in which all of the final games kick off at once (whoa). When all was said and done, Gotham FC won the NWSL Shield—more on that in a minute—and all remaining teams locked in their seeding for playoffs.

So, which teams are in contention and how is this all going to go down? Whether you've never seen a NWSL game or need to brush up on the facts, we've got you covered.

The NWSL hosts single-elimination playoffs, which means if a team loses once, they're out. You can compare this to the NFL, whereas leagues like the WNBA, NBA, NHL, and MLB play a best-of series in each round to give teams multiple chances to advance.

This is a little confusing, so bear with us. The NWSL Shield is a regular-season honor that culminates at the end of the year.

The Shield is given to the team that has the best record over the regular season, which began back in March. Now, the playoffs begin, and the winner of the playoff tournament wins the NWSL Championship. It's possible to win both the Shield and the Championship, btw. It's just that the Shield is a distinction for regular season play. (Gotham FC won the NWSL Championship in 2023 and 2025, but only won the club’s first-ever Shield this season.)

The team that wins the Shield (in this case, Gotham) is also granted the No. 1 seed in the playoffs, which means they get home-field advantage during the postseason.

If there's a tie in the NWSL playoffs, the game will go on to extra time. This is two 15-minute periods that are played in their entirety. Whoever has the highest score at the end wins.

If things are still tied at that point, this goes to a penalty shootout. This is a best of five round of kicks, followed by sudden-death rounds if they're needed.

The first round of the playoffs will be broadcast on ESPN platforms, along with Prime Video, CBS, and Paramount+. The semifinals, which take place November 14-15, will be broadcast on ESPN networks, CBS, and Paramount+. The championship game will air on CBS and Paramount+.

You might even consider visiting a dedicated women’s sports bar in your area to watch with other like-minded fans—here’s an exhaustive list.

Eight teams are in contention this year. Here they are, as well as their seeds going into the first round:

The championship game is on Saturday, Nov. 21 at 8 pm ET. Last year’s championship game saw Gotham FC beat the Washington Spirt 1-0 with a goal at the 80th minute. The stage is set for an equally exciting finale to the NWSL season.

#5044. Elon Musk Calls Claude Opus 5.5 a 'Masterpiece', Admits Grok 4.8 Won't Match It

Elon Musk Calls Claude Opus 5.5 a 'Masterpiece', Admits Grok 4.8 Won't Match It

Elon Musk has given a rival AI model an unusually enthusiastic endorsement while acknowledging that his own next release won't be able to beat it. Asked how Grok 4.8 would compare with Anthropic's Claude Opus 5.5, the xAI founder answered it would be “not as good.”

“Opus 5.5 is a masterpiece,” Musk posted on X.

The admission came in a reply on X Tuesday to a question from a user named Dimdv. Musk added that Grok 5, a next generation model that will be released after this update, “should exceed Opus 5.5,” potentially in two or three months, although he gave further details for that prediction or a firm date for its release—and he’s known for not sticking to his own target dates.

For a company trying to catch up with Anthropic and OpenAI, the distinction matters. Grok 4.8 is the update Musk expects to put in some users' hands this week; Grok 5 is the model he believes could eventually put his company ahead, until competing AI companies release a new model.

The exchange began with a technical update in which Musk said Grok 4.8 had finished pretraining but was still undergoing reinforcement learning, or RL, a phase in which a model's responses are refined using feedback and rewards. He said the team had also spent considerable time cleaning up supervised fine-tuning data, the examples used to teach the model how to follow instructions.

Musk said the company had abandoned JAX, a machine-learning framework, and SGLang, software used to serve AI models, in favor of an inference and reinforcement-learning system rewritten in C and C++. Inference is the process of generating answers after a user sends a prompt; rewriting the underlying software can offer engineers more control over performance, though Musk provided no figures showing the resulting gains.

Developer tetsuo celebrated the announcement with “C wins again,” prompting Musk to clarify that the code was “C with a little C++.” He subsequently credited Starlink engineers with work that helped produce the Grok training and inference stack, pointing to SpaceX's practice of writing its own headers and handling precise time calculations, including leap seconds.

Musk described Grok 4.8 as “not yet incredible” but said its foundations were finally built correctly. He expects a soft rollout to Bot users later this week, giving the company an early test before any broader release.

Anthropic released Opus 5.5 on September 22, delivering a performance comparable to its higher-end Fable 5.1 model on most work while costing 40% less to run than the previous Opus version on typical workloads. The model targets difficult programming jobs and tasks in which AI agents work through multiple steps with limited human assistance.

On Terminal-Bench 4.0, which tests whether AI agents can complete complicated tasks in a command-line environment and scores them by the percentage successfully finished, Anthropic reported 66.4% for Opus 5.5 versus 57.9% for OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra. Grok is very far behind in the leaderboards.

Musk has not published Grok 4.8 scores that would establish how large the gap really is. His immediate target is the limited rollout later this week; the promised Grok 5 challenge to Opus 5.5 is still, by his own estimate, two or three months away.

#5042. Stablecoin supply drops $14.2 billion in largest contraction since 2022 bear market

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

USDT and USDC shed nearly $11 billion in supply over the past month as analysts flag less buying power for a crypto rebound.

Crypto's pool of stablecoins is shrinking at its fastest pace in more than four years, alongside a stalled market recovery

A Wednesday report from Glassnode found that the supply of major stablecoins fell by $14.2 billion over the past 30 days, the biggest drop since the 2022 crypto bear market. For comparison, the 30-day stablecoin supply decline nearly reached $30 billion in June 2022, roughly five months before bitcoin reached its bear market low of $15,500 that November.

The total stablecoin supply now sits just below $280 billion, levels not seen since September 2025.

"A shrinking supply means holders are redeeming stablecoins for dollars and taking that cash out of the market, which leaves less fuel for a rally," Glassnode wrote.

A separate report from CryptoQuant on Tuesday focused on the deteriorating supply of Tether's USDT, the long-time stablecoin market leader. Its market capitalization fell $6.6 billion over the 60 days through Nov. 7, more than twice the $3.2 billion drop over a similar period in December 2022 following the collapse of the FTX exchange.

USDT currently accounts for roughly 62.8% of stablecoin supply, followed by Circle's USDC at 25.1%, according to The Block's data.

Gold-backed tokens have held steady by comparison, with a combined market cap of $5.61 billion. That remains below February's $6.31 billion peak when gold was at an all-time high of around $5,350 per troy ounce.

Glassnode said that a return to stablecoin supply growth would be the "first sign that new money is coming in." Meanwhile, CryptoQuant said a local bitcoin bottom could form before USDT supply flips back to positive territory.

#5041. Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI Breach Exposes User Details and Data From 18 AI Agents

Near AI has disclosed a breach that exposed account information belonging to about 100 users and data from 18 AI agents after an attacker exploited software running its servers.

The company said in a November 9 report that an attacker used a remote code execution vulnerability in SGLang, an open-source engine that runs AI models and generates responses to user requests.

The exploit allowed the intruder to execute commands on one inference server and then reach other parts of Near AI’s infrastructure.

Investigators traced the intrusion to an internal system connected to Private Chat, a feature the company had already retired. The attacker downloaded names and email addresses for approximately 100 users, along with subscription plan and account balance information for some of them.

Another 18 agents were accessed on an older deployment of Near AI Agents, where the attacker retrieved their chat histories, settings and logs. Those agents were not running inside NEAR AI’s confidential computing environment, which is designed to shield information from the company operating the servers.

Logs also exposed NEAR AI Cloud API keys belonging to two customers. These keys function like digital credentials that applications use to access cloud services; Near AI says it has revoked both, but did not say whether the attacker used them.

Near AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files had been accessed. The confirmed theft involved account records and legacy agent data, while exposure of live confidential requests remains a possibility identified by the investigation.

Hardware isolation does not guarantee that the software inside the protected environment is safe. “It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment,” Near AI acknowledged, explaining that the SGLang flaw let the attacker run code where confidential requests were being processed.

SGLang had already attracted scrutiny from security researchers. In July, Carnegie Mellon University’s CERT Coordination Center documented six vulnerabilities in the project, including bugs that could let attackers execute commands or expose credentials, depending on how servers were configured.

Near AI did not identify the specific SGLang flaw exploited in this incident, so there is no basis to link the breach to any of those earlier disclosures. It reported the vulnerability to SGLang’s developers.

Near, to its credit, was quick to fix the issues.

“On detection, we revoked all active sessions and administrative credentials, replaced all agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and deployed a fix for the SGLang vulnerability across all inference servers,” the project said.

”We've completed the rotation of all remaining credentials and a full forensic review. We have notified users whose account details or agents we have confirmed were accessed and will make any regulatory notifications that are required.”

Near AI notified users whose account records or agents it confirmed were accessed and says it will make any required regulatory notifications. It also warned users to be wary of unsolicited messages referring to their Near AI accounts or balances, and contacted the owners of all 18 affected agents individually.

#5037. Sen. Warren, Merkley raise ethics concerns over Trump aide’s prediction market bets
