#4959. Paraguay bank collapse shows stablecoins can offer a lifeline... of sorts

Ueno’s collapse exposed the fragility of digital banking in Paraguay, as customers scrambled for access to their money and stablecoins offered a lifeline.

The collapse of Paraguay’s digital lender Ueno Bank last week left nearly three million customers — roughly 60% of Paraguay’s adults — unable to access their accounts.

Paraguay’s Central Bank placed Ueno into resolution on Nov. 3 after its solvency ratio fell below half the legally required minimum. An investigation uncovered questionable lending practices and deficiencies in credit documentation and accounting.

The collapse also triggered a political crisis, with thousands taking to the streets to protest and President Santiago Peña attempting to resign before being impeached and arrested on charges of breach of trust and using his office for personal gain.

Paraguay’s peer-to-peer crypto market immediately reflected the turmoil, with some sellers asking for a USDt premium of up to 13%. “USDt is a lifeline for hundreds millions of people in the world,” Tether chief executive Paolo Ardoino said.

For many, the incident sparked uncertainty over savings, wages and the money needed to cover everyday expenses. With the local guaraní (PYG) currency falling 12.3% in a week, some turned to stablecoins to maintain the value of their funds, or just to keep payments moving along.

David Eisenmann, founder and chief executive of Paraguayan crypto exchange X4T, tells Magazine the timing made the liquidity need even more pronounced.

“The intervention came in the first days of the month,” he says, “exactly when salaries, rent and supplier invoices are due,” adding that many business owners “couldn’t make payroll.” For some, USDt was “suddenly the most liquid money” they had:

But beyond another TradFi masterclass in the virtues of self-custody, Ueno’s collapse highlights the growing role of dollar-pegged stablecoins providing an alternative source of liquidity when traditional banks fail.

Eisenmann says X4T processed more than $10 million in local on-ramp volume over the 30 days to early November, but some of that activity predated Ueno’s collapse.

“The dollar began recovering against the guaraní in October,” he says, “and the Ueno news sped up a move that was already underway.”

Related: Stablecoins can drain from banks and nations at lightning speed

He says the activity “runs in both directions,” with some customers selling crypto for guaraníes or dollars to cover payments they could no longer make through Ueno, while “demand for USDt and USDC as a dollar store of value has risen sharply.”

Paraguay’s Central Bank placed Ueno into resolution on Nov. 3. Source: Central Bank of Paraguay

As of Nov. 9 the Central Bank said 93.1% of customers with Ueno deposits had been repaid. Despite this confidence has been shaken and X4T continued to see higher online inquiries for dollar-denominated stablecoins. In fact, Eisenmann says the level “has not changed significantly” from the week of the collapse.

Rhys Hicks, associate analyst at data and analytics company GlobalData, says the digital bank’s failure has shown “how much can go wrong when governance does not keep pace with growth.”

That said, he’s not seeing evidence of a mass migration from Paraguayan banks into stablecoins, since other banks “picked up the slack,” and people “seem to have been angrier” with the government than with the banks.

“Some consumers will look for different ways to keep their money safe, but I do not see an overwhelming shift to stablecoins yet,” he says.

Seeking dollars when confidence in domestic banks falters is hardly new. In Argentina’s 2001 financial crisis, the government imposed the “corralito,” restricting cash withdrawals from bank accounts, following a run on deposits.

Related: Peter Thiel backs crypto-friendly Silicon Valley Bank rival — Report

Amid a deepening crisis over the country’s currency peg and sovereign debt, the government later converted dollar-denominated deposits into pesos, reducing people’s savings by about 30% overnight and further damaging confidence in the country’s fraying banking system.

Tether CEO calls USDt a lifeline. Source: Paolo Ardoino

But stablecoins aren’t decentralized and they are not insulated from the banking system either.

During the March 2023 collapse of Silicon Valley Bank, stablecoin issuer Circle disclosed that $3.3 billion of USDC reserves, around 8% of the total, were held at the lender. USDC briefly depegged and fell to 86 cents on secondary markets after Circle said it could not withdraw the funds.

A BIS paper on stablecoins and the international monetary system warns that widespread use of dollar-backed tokens could create challenges for monetary sovereignty in emerging markets.

Hicks says stablecoins can provide an alternative during periods of political instability and “low trust in the local system” because they are pegged to a more stable currency, like the US dollar.

But regulators need to address “transparency” and “consumer protection,” he says, because stablecoin holders don’t benefit from the same deposit guarantees as bank customers, and there is no central bank to step in and make them whole.

Eisenmann says he expects the incident to spark more “diversification,” with people “spreading their money across banks, cash and digital assets” instead of relying on a single institution.

“How quickly trust in the banks returns will depend on how quickly and transparently the authorities handle the resolution,” he says.

#4956. Deutsche Bank Goes Live With Crypto Custody After a Five-Year Buildup

Deutsche Bank Goes Live With Crypto Custody After a Five-Year Buildup

Five years after it first floated the idea, Germany’s largest lender is now holding Bitcoin for clients.

Deutsche Bank said Wednesday that its Digital Asset Custody service has gone live for paying clients, giving institutional and corporate customers in Europe a regulated way to store and transfer Bitcoin, Ethereum and three stablecoins: Circle’s USDC and EURC, plus EURAU.

The bank manages wallets and private keys on clients’ behalf. For now, the service covers custody and transfers, with tokenized financial instruments on its roadmap.

The service is aimed at clients of Deutsche Bank’s corporate and investment banking units, including corporations, asset managers, hedge funds, custodians, brokers and sovereign institutions, according to the bank’s September announcement of the plan.

Its setup includes hardware-based key protection, multi-person approvals and separate warm and cold storage, the bank said.

“In the medium term we aim to deliver a fully integrated, regulated ecosystem that supports the custody, tokenisation, and settlement of digital assets,” said Paul Maley, Deutsche Bank’s global head of trust and securities services.

Deutsche Bank also signed a memorandum of understanding with Deutsche Asset Management, the majority-owned money manager formerly known as DWS, to explore working together on tokenized money market funds, crypto exchange-traded commodities and stablecoins.

The asset manager oversaw about 1.22 trillion euros as of Sept. 30. The bank has also said it plans to expand the custody service to Asia.

The launch caps a long road, going back to at least 2021 when Deutsche Bank began readying a crypto custody and trading platform. Then, in 2023, the bank applied for a digital asset custody license in Germany. Last year, reports said the bank planned to build the service with technology from crypto exchange Bitpanda, alongside its longtime partner Taurus.

Deutsche Bank received authorization from German regulator BaFin under the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets regulation, or MiCA, which requires crypto service providers to be licensed. The bank cautioned that its custody service doesn’t protect clients against swings in the value of digital assets, fraud or failures by other market participants.

The custody launch isn’t Deutsche Bank’s only crypto bet. The bank is among a group of global lenders, including Goldman Sachs and Citi, backing a $270 million challenge to crypto’s dominant stablecoin issuers.

#4955. 7 Best Oushak Rugs Fit for Royalty, Vetted by AD (2026)

Few home furnishings can make an ordinary room feel quite so grand as Oushak rugs. Named for the Turkish town of Uşak, where the weaving tradition began, these hand-knotted floor coverings gained popularity during the Ottoman Empire and were eventually exported across Europe. Their designs are recognizable for their large-scale medallions, geometric borders, and floral motifs, many of which drew inspiration from Ottoman art and architecture. The result is a style that’s decorative but never overly fussy, which helps to explain how it has withstood the test of time.

An Oushak rug can sit comfortably beneath a traditional dining table, add some contrast to a pared-back living room, or bring some personality to an otherwise neutral bedroom. And while antique examples are certainly worth seeking out, plenty of modern Oushak rugs capture the same old-world grandeur. Put simply, you may not be a sultan, but you can certainly decorate like one.

This one-of-a-kind, 100% wool rug is hand-knotted in Taşpinar, Turkey. Its intricate pattern, light pink palette, and low-pile height of half a centimeter give it a lived-in feel that works particularly well alongside more contemporary furniture. The wool is naturally durable, so even with a little wear, the rug has plenty of life left in it.

Bring a little more floral to your floors with this lavender Oushak. Its vintage-inspired pattern is full of the botanical motifs that make Turkish rugs so distinctive, while the soft color protects its relaxed aura from feeling overly ornate. Hand-knotted from New Zealand wool, the floor covering adds just enough pattern to a room without taking over. Basically, it’s the kind of rug that makes you want to kick off your shoes and stay awhile.

This cotton-candy-hued Oushak rug, another flower-filled masterpiece, features scattered palmettes and geometric shapes that draw on the rich history of Turkish patternmaking. Handmade by artisans in India from a blend of New Zealand wool, cotton, and polyester, and available in a variety of sizes to fit many spaces, the rug’s plush feel and storied patina make it a beautiful foundation for any room.

This Oushak-inspired rug pairs symmetrical designs with a sky-blue palette, bringing brightness to any room. Think of it as artwork you can touch or, rather, walk all over. Simply place it down and let the compliments roll in. It’s meticulously handwoven from high-quality wool, making it a durable choice for high-traffic areas, and because it comes in several sizes, its an especially versatile choice.

If you’re searching for a floor covering that has the essence of an antique Persian rug, but you aren’t quite ready to worry about spills, look no further. While this option features a delicate floral pattern, its tufted polyester construction makes it a more resilient selection for the busier corners of your home. It’s machine washable up to the 8-foot-by-10-foot size (larger versions are still washable but may require a commercial machine), and is available in area rug, round, and runner formats. It’s a particularly good pick for busy households where a beautiful rug has to do more than just look pretty.

For those who prefer more subtle patterns, this simple but sophisticated hand-knotted Oushak rug is a lovely option. Its understated mix of cream, brown, green, and blue complements a soft, slightly faded finish that gives it the appeal of a vintage find.

This Oushak rug looks like it belongs to another millennium and should live in the Metropolitan Museum of Art with its perfectly faded medallion pattern and ornamental border. But it’s actually brand new—and an affordable option for just about any home. Its muted palette can skew bohemian or coastal, or simply bring a little intrigue to a cozy space of any style.

#4953. Deutsche Bank launches crypto custody for companies and institutions

Clients can store and transfer Bitcoin, Ether and select stablecoins through the bank, which manages their wallets and private keys.

Germany’s largest lender, Deutsche Bank, launched its crypto custody service for institutional and corporate clients on Wednesday, according to an announcement shared with Cointelegraph.

The bank also signed a memorandum of understanding with Deutsche Asset Management, its majority-owned asset manager, to explore collaboration on tokenized money market funds, crypto asset exchange-traded commodities and stablecoins.

Deutsche Bank announced plans to offer the service to institutional and corporate clients in Europe on Sept. 16, pending regulatory clearance. Deutsche Bank’s listing for crypto custody and transfer services in the European Union’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA) register is dated Oct. 12 and names Germany’s BaFin as its regulator.

The service initially supports Bitcoin (BTC), Ether (ETH) and select stablecoins, including Circle’s USDC (USDC) and EURC (EURC), as well as AllUnity EURAU.

Gerald Podobnik, co-head of Deutsche Bank’s corporate bank, told Reuters in October that the bank planned to launch in Germany before expanding to Asian financial centers in 2027.

Deutsche Bank partnered with crypto infrastructure provider Taurus in 2023 to develop custody services after applying for a digital asset custody license in Germany.

#4950. Bitcoin hovers near $74K as stocks rise despite US bond yield rebound

Bitcoin traded in a narrow range as investors bought the dip in tech stocks and US bond yields climbed on high CPI inflation data.

Bitcoin (BTC) circled $74,000 at Wednesday’s Wall Street open as stock markets rallied and US bond yields surged.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD diverging from US equities as investors resumed buying the dip in tech stocks.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

The AI sector led the day’s early gains, with cloud-computing company CoreWeave jumping over 20% after reporting better-than-expected Q3 revenue and lifting its 2026 guidance.

CoreWeave stock one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Nvidia, which suffered on Monday and Tuesday amid ongoing controversy over CEO Jensen Huang, opened around 2.5% higher.

Nvidia stock one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Oil prices, meanwhile, gained on the day amid fading optimism over a diplomatic solution to the US-Iran war. As attacks on ships traversing the Strait of Hormuz continued, analysis pointed to several practical hurdles to restoring pre-conflict traffic levels, even if a deal is reached.

“Even in the event a deal is struck, an increase in flows through the waterway would still depend on mine clearance, insurance costs and whether or not shipowners would even be willing to take the risk,” LSEG Oil Research wrote in a note on Tuesday, according to Reuters.

US WTI crude oil was up 1.4% on the day at $87.70 per barrel after dropping to $84 on Tuesday, its lowest since late August. Brent crude gained 1.5% to $93.40.

CFDs on WTI crude oil one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Stocks rose even after Tuesday’s higher-than-expected Consumer Price Index (CPI) inflation data pushed US bond yields sharply higher. The 10-year yield returned to 5% on Wednesday for the first time since Oct. 29, while the 30-year yield reached as high as 5.378%, a level last seen on Oct. 22.

US 10-year bond yield one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Peter Boockvar, chief investment officer at One Point BFG Wealth Partners, said that bonds were pricing in a more hawkish approach to interest rates by the US Federal Reserve. Markets have priced in a 0.25% rate hike in December, with the odds of a 0.5% move now increasing.

“Inflation breakevens today are focused on the drop in oil prices while regular Treasuries today are reacting to a Fed that is definitely going to hike next month, as of now, with one another in January,” Boockvar told CNBC.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

Bitcoin continued to trade in a narrow range between its 21-week and 50-week simple moving average at $73,078 and $75,492, respectively. Volatility cooled as traders awaited a new catalyst after the CPI report.

BTC/USD one-day chart with 21-week and 50-week SMAs. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Investor profitability suffered as Bitcoin lost support around $75,700, the aggregate cost basis of the short-term holder (STH) cohort - entities holding a given amount of BTC without selling for up to 155 days.

On Tuesday, STH wallets sent over 47,000 BTC to exchanges at an unrealized loss, potentially signaling an increase in panic selling if those coins were sold.

Give your home a floor-to-ceiling revamp, starting with this three-piece pendant light. The dark gray powder-coating coordinates with other dark metals. Install it over a wooden dining room table for contrast, or over a marble kitchen island to further modernize the space.

This amply cushioned boucle-upholstered sectional is made for lounging and storing any overflow items. In a New York City apartment, for instance, we’ve found that these hidden storage spaces can serve as a bonus “closet” for clothing, bedding, or towels. We also love the modularity of the design, allowing you to move the ottoman to the right or left side as needed.

There’s a whimsical yet architectural nature to this accent chair. The oversized side panels give your arms a place to rest, and the playful design is reinforced with the brand’s recommendation to use the removable cushions to build your own pillow fort. It’s a versatile piece that still feels distinct and fun.

#4948. Tether's Tiny AI Agent Beats Models 2.5 Times Its Size at Controlling Your Phone

Tether's Tiny AI Agent Beats Models 2.5 Times Its Size at Controlling Your Phone

Tether, the company behind the world's largest stablecoin, USDT, is getting surprisingly good at making tiny AI models punch above their weight.

The company on Wednesday unveiled QVAC BotPsy, an 800-million-parameter AI agent designed to carry out everyday tasks on personal devices. Parameters are essentially the knobs that determine how much an AI model can do, and more of them generally translate to improved performance. And for reference, frontier AI models today run in the trillions of parameters.

In the company's own testing, Tether’s relatively tiny model outperformed competitors with up to 2.5 times as many parameters, suggesting that AI assistants may not need enormous models to get things done.

Unlike traditional chatbots that simply respond to questions, BotPsy is agentic, meaning it’s designed to take action. It can identify which tools an application needs, execute commands in the right order, and adjust when something goes wrong.

For example, Tether described a benchmark scenario in which an AI assistant needed to send a message but encountered two problems: Wi-Fi was disabled and the inbox was full. BotPsy turned on Wi-Fi, cleared enough storage, and completed the task, while the underlying model repeatedly attempted the same failed operation.

That's a fairly mundane achievement, but it's precisely the kind of problem that makes AI agents useful outside a chatbot window.

BotPsy is built on Alibaba's Qwen3.5-0.8B architecture, a relatively small language model that Tether trained further to specialize in interacting with software tools. Its 800 million parameters represent the numerical values the model learned during training, and a smaller parameter count generally means less memory is needed to run it.

According to Tether's technical documentation, BotPsy scored 44.0% on BFCL v4, a benchmark that evaluates an AI system's ability to select and execute software functions across different tasks and conversations. Scores reflect a weighted percentage of successful performance across tool-use categories.

The underlying Qwen3.5-0.8B model scored 23.4%, while the considerably larger Qwen3.5-2B reached 37.7%.

BotPsy also scored 58.7% on ACEBench, which evaluates the accuracy of function calls, including selecting the appropriate tools and arguments. Its base model managed 30.1%, while the 2-billion-parameter competitor scored 55.4%.

The company tested five models across six tool-use evaluations, with BotPsy posting the highest results on five. Its average score across those tests reached 45.0%, compared with 38.8% for the larger Qwen model.

BotPsy is the latest addition to QVAC, Tether's effort to build an AI ecosystem that runs on devices controlled by users rather than relying entirely on centralized cloud infrastructure.

The company first introduced QVAC in May 2025, positioning the project as an alternative to AI services operated by technology giants. Its longer-term ambitions included autonomous agents capable of interacting with applications and potentially making payments using Bitcoin and USDT.

Tether expanded that initiative in October 2025 with a 41-billion-token dataset for training science and engineering models, alongside QVAC Workbench, an application that lets users run AI models locally.

Earlier this year, Tether released its SDK, providing developers with tools to build offline AI applications across mobile and desktop operating systems.

The company also developed QVAC MedPsy, a medical AI system designed to run on consumer hardware. Its 1.7-billion-parameter version outperformed Google's much larger MedGemma-27B on HealthBench Hard, a test of realistic clinical conversations evaluated using physician-developed criteria.

BotPsy is currently available as an open-weight model under the Apache 2.0 license, meaning developers can download, modify, and integrate it into their own applications. Tether has also released compressed versions intended to reduce memory requirements for local deployment.

The company has not announced a release date for a finished consumer assistant powered by BotPsy. Its published model card also says the training code and training data are not included in the release.

#4947. Acne Scars, Begone

These dermatologist-approved treatments can get your skin smooth again, reduce hyperpigmentation, and may even make you look a little younger.

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

ACNE SUCKS. THE scars it can leave behind suck even more. These dermatologist-approved* treatments can get your skin smooth again, reduce hyperpigmentation, and may even make you look a little younger.

These are known as atrophic scars and are caused when inflammation breaks down skin’s collagen that’s responsible for smoothness and elasticity. There are three main forms of atrophic scars, and some people will develop a mix of all of them.

Meet the icepick scar, so named because it looks as if your skin was pierced with something thin and pointed.

Derms will typically combine topical trichloroacetic acid (TCA) with a CO2 laser to supercharge the production of new collagen and resurface skin. Often, up to three sessions are needed and typically range in cost from $300 to $5,000 per session, depending on the size of the scar(s) and whether treatments are combined.

Commonly referred to as boxcar scars, this type of scar doesn’t run as deep as their icepick brethren and may respond to less invasive treatments.

The Treatment: Fraxel FTX or Microneedling

Your doctor may use a slightly more superficial fractionated laser (like the new nonablative Fraxel FTX). This procedure is around $1,000 per session. Another option is a microneedling treatment (tiny needles penetrate the skin to kick-start collagen), which can be paired with heat-like radiofrequency for better results. Multiple sessions at $600 to $1,500 a pop are typically necessary.

These rolling scars are slightly different, caused, yes, by collagen loss, but also when acne inflammation tethers areas of skin to the tissue below.

One option is a technique called subcision (which ranges anywhere from $300 to $1,500)—your doc will cut away the tethers pulling the skin downward to release the scar. Another option is Bellafill (roughly $800 per syringe), a type of filler that adds volume to depressed areas, or hyaluronic acid fillers (roughly $650 per syringe), which also lifts depressed areas to the same level as the surrounding skin.

You’re dealing with a hypertrophic scar or a keloid, which typically result from severe, deep cystic or nodular acne.

The hallmark signs of a standard hypertrophic scar, these signal that your body’s response to injury is to churn out way more collagen than you need. These scars are more common among people with darker skin tones.

Corticosteroid injections (roughly $100 to $300 per injection) flatten and soften excess scar tissue. A fractional CO2 laser breaks up the collagen, reduces the size of the scars, and helps injected or applied medications penetrate more effectively.

Think of this—called a keloid—as a hypertrophic scar on steroids. An apropos analogy, as that’s what you’ll use to treat it, too.

Corticosteroid injections will most likely be needed to break apart this large, fibrous collection of collagen tissue, but CO2 or fractionated lasers can be used afterward to improve the skin’s texture.

*Reviewed by Bradley Glodny, MD, and Rishi Chopra, MD.

#4946. How to Train for a 5K, According to a Running Coach

And how to run your next one even faster.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

There's a reason runners love the 5K distance. It's not too long, but it's not too short, either. A 5K is just a little over three miles (3.1, to be exact), and it’s actually a great distance for runners of all ages, fitness levels, and experience levels, according to Erica Coviello, owner and running coach at Run Fit Stoked.

“I love a 5K because typically they're smaller races, so they really foster a sense of community, not just for runners, but anybody in the community,” she says. They’re also held pretty frequently, so if something goes wrong with training, the stakes aren’t as high as missing a once-a-year marathon.

Whether your goal is to run the full distance without stopping, achieve a certain time, or jump in on your family’s turkey trot, the right training plan will set you up for success. A 5K is an aerobic challenge, says Coviello. “That means you want to be able to get your heart in the condition to pump oxygen efficiently,” she says. By balancing different types of runs with cross-training, strength training, and rest days, a plan can make sure you’re working—but not overworking—your muscles while getting your heart into the right condition.

A good 5K training plan incorporates the right mix of workouts and (thankfully) plenty of rest days too. Read on for your running coach-approved training schedule and some tips to crush your next 5K.

Meet the experts: Erica Coviello, CPT, is a level 2 RRCA running coach and owner of Run Fit Stoked. Claudette Sariya, CPT, is a personal trainer, fitness coach, and organizer of Asian Women Stay Running (AWSTARS) Run Club in NYC.

In Coviello’s 5K training plan, you’ll typically be running four days a week and resting for three. Take a look at the chart for all the details, and keep scrolling for a breakdown of all the kinds of runs you’ll be doing.

Easy runs should be what they sound like: easy. They should be getting you to about 60 percent of your max heart rate, though you don’t need to focus on getting to an exact number, says Coviello. It’s more about the feel.

“Easy is when you're not huffing and puffing and it's not difficult to take a breath,” says Coviello. “Even if you're running alone, you should be able to have a full conversation with a person running next to you without having to gasp for breath every couple of words.”

This is what runners call a conversational pace. And, by the way, a majority of your runs in this training plan are easy.

Your long runs—done just once a week—should also be at a conversational pace, and they’re actually the most important run of all because they really train your muscles, connective tissue, and everything else you need to keep up with what you’re training your heart to do, says Coviello.

If a long run seems daunting to you, Coviello has two helpful tips. The first is that it is totally okay to walk if you need to take breaks. You’re still moving forward, after all. If you find that you need to walk a lot, that’s also potentially a sign that you’re starting out too fast. The second tip: Do your long run with a friend. Bringing someone else along not only keeps you entertained but is also a good way to tell if you’re running at a conversational pace, because, well, you should be able to have a conversation with them.

Depending on the pace that’s comfortable for you, you might even find that you run farther than a 5K during your long runs. That’s a good thing, says Coviello. It will make sure your endurance is there on race day.

These days are less important for beginners (and can actually just be treated as easy days if you don’t have a time goal), but are the key for anyone who wants to run a 5K at a certain pace.

On each weekly speed run, you’ll rotate between running at a faster pace and a slower one. By increasing your speed for shorter amounts of time, you get your body and mind used to going at your goal race pace until you can do it for the entire time. “It just adds on a little bit each time so that by the time you get to that 5K, you're ready to run the whole race at that pace,” says Coviello.

Since the speed workout format may be new for some, here's an example of what it could look like:

You can take all three days on this plan as true rest days, but there’s also the option to do a form of cross-training too. Cross-training should be another form of aerobic exercise. Think swimming, biking, using the elliptical—any form of low-impact cardio that you enjoy works. And, remember to take one full day for solely rest. Your body needs it!

Strength training should be done two to three times a week, and only needs to take about 20 minutes, says Coviello. You can do strength training after a run or at least two hours before a run. You're going to want to focus on the most important muscles for running when you're strength training.

They include your legs, glutes, calves, and core, says Claudette Sariya, CPT, a personal trainer, fitness coach, and organizer of the Asian Women Stay Running Run Club in NYC. You can keep it simple and do bodyweight exercises, but adding resistance is the only way to grow your muscles, she adds.

The answer to this question is going to be different for everybody, says Coviello. Generally, if you shoot for a 30-minute 5K, that means you’re running slightly faster than a 10-minute mile, which is a great goal for newer runners. But, the beauty of this distance is that it can be done much faster or slower than at a ten-minute pace. “Walkers can do this too,” Coviello says.

Coviello’s plan is 8 weeks long, but you can likely speed up or slow down this training plan depending on your experience level. For example, if you’re already running a 5K distance but looking to shave off some time, you won’t need eight weeks to see results. If you’re a true beginner, you might want to give yourself more time—like 12 to 16 weeks instead—to really get your body used to the idea of moving quickly, Coviello says.

Her eight-week plan is a great starting point for those who already have a little bit of an aerobic base (a.k.a. you already do some running, walking, swimming, or other kinds of fitness activities).

Aside from the musts like a good pair of running sneakers, a lot of the running gear you’ll need is weather-dependent. In warmer weather, Coviello recommends light, moisture-wicking clothes. Plus, you’ll want to stay protected from the sun by wearing a hat or sunglasses and sunscreen. In colder weather, warm socks (like Smartwool), something to keep your ears and head warm, and lots of layers will be important.

In order to follow Coviello’s plan, you’ll also want some sort of timing and tracking device.

The best way to prevent injury is by getting a good warm-up in, and it only takes about 5 to 10 minutes. Some dynamic stretching and a slow jog or walk will help wake up your muscles before a run.

One thing you should skip in your warm-up? Static stretching, says Coviello. This might actually make you more prone to getting an injury and is best done after a run.

Additionally, at any point in your training, you shouldn’t push through pain. If something hurts, you should take a rest (which is often what your body needs to remedy the issue, says Coviello), but if pain continues, you should consult a physical therapist or doctor.

She reports, writes, and edits content ranging from service stories to in-depth features and awards packages across all verticals, including health, fitness, sports, and beauty. While at WH, she’s traveled to Paris, France to watch Faith Kipyegon attempt a sub-4-minute mile and covered major races like Ultra Trail du Mont Blanc in Chamonix. Olivia is a RRCA-certified running coach who is especially passionate about helping people start their running journey and is training for her second marathon. Her works also appears in New York Magazine’s The Cut, PS (formerly Popsugar), and Salon, where she also did on-camera interviews with celebrity guests.

#4945. OKXICE moves ahead with tokenized stocks as blue chips push back

The Nov. 23 debut puts a new SEC exemption to the test as Nasdaq pursues its own tokenized stock plans after warning that trading across platforms could fragment liquidity.

Crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE) are set to launch tokenized stock trading on Nov. 23, despite objections from 67 companies to having their shares listed and warnings from Nasdaq that trading across disconnected platforms could fragment liquidity.

The venture, known as OKXICE, will operate under the US Securities and Exchange Commission’s temporary Innovation Exemption and offer eligible US customers round-the-clock trading. Under the exemption, venues must give companies a chance to object before listing shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. JPMorgan Chase, McDonald’s, SpaceX and Coca-Cola were among the companies that objected.

Tokenized stocks are blockchain-based tokens that represent company shares or track their price. The rights they carry depend on how they are structured. The distributed value of tokenized stocks and ETFs reached $3.88 billion on Nov. 11, up 13.4% over 30 days.

In comments to Cointelegraph, former New York governor and OKXICE co-chair Andrew Cuomo said investors would have “the same rights, protections and ownership that come with the underlying shares.”

OKX expects the venue to list more than 70 assets, including 59 exchange-traded funds (ETFs) and tokenized shares of Coinbase, Circle, BitGo, Robinhood and Strategy. The lineup also includes Strategy’s preferred shares.

Related: Public companies object to OKX-ICE’s tokenized stock plan

The rollout comes amid concerns that trading the same shares across traditional exchanges and blockchain platforms could split liquidity and make prices less reliable.

In October, a study by the International Monetary Fund found that the tokenized stocks were about 1.5 times as volatile as the same stocks in traditional markets. Decentralized exchanges had the weakest liquidity and their prices strayed further from those in traditional markets than prices on centralized exchanges did.

The IMF also warned that combining trading and settlement on a blockchain could remove safeguards that help markets absorb shocks. As tokenized markets grow, greater use of leverage and closer links between markets could amplify fire sales, liquidity runs and financial contagion.

Nasdaq argued in a January filing that trading tokenized stocks on platforms outside traditional rails could fragment liquidity and make it harder for investors to get the best available price. Regular US stock trading runs from 9:30 a.m. to 4 p.m. Eastern time on weekdays, excluding market holidays, though some brokers offer premarket, after-hours and overnight trading.

Cuomo described the current market structure was “built for another era,” arguing that crypto markets had already shown that trading could run around the clock. “Now we are bringing that standard to equities, without asking investors to settle for synthetic exposure or give up their shareholder rights,” he said.

#4944. How to Treat Acne Like an Adult

Adult acne suuucks. We’re here to help you treat it (no Stridex pads in sight).

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

EVERYONE KNOWS NOT to squeeze a pimple, so what’s a grown-ass man supposed to do to clear his acne? Consider this your official guide to treating breakouts at home or with the help of a dermatologist.

Below are the most effective ingredients available in over-the-counter (OTC) acne products. Use ’em daily to keep breakouts at bay and combine them for the best results…provided your skin doesn’t freak out. Whether you use an ingredient in a cleanser, toner, serum, moisturizer, or as a targeted spot treatment depends on what your skin can handle.

As a beta-hydroxy acid (BHA), salicylic acid gently exfoliates dead skin cells and dissolves excess sebum that can clog pores, says Joshua Zeichner, MD, a dermatologist at Mount Sinai Hospital. It can be effective in facial cleansers, toners, serums, moisturizers, or spot treatments, but a body wash is ideal for larger areas like the back or chest. You’ll spy the ingredient in 0.5% to 2% concentrations, though the latter is considered most effective for acne-prone skin.

BP is a winner because it “lowers the levels of acne-causing bacteria and has some anti-inflammatory effects,” Dr. Zeichner explains. It can be irritating, so use a lower percentage (2.5% to 10% is the FDA-approved range) for a leave-on product, and a higher concentration for a rinse-off product.

Try: Neutrogena Evenly Clear Acne Cleansing Mask

No, you won’t smell like rotten eggs—and if you think of yourself as the sensitive type, sulfur (at 3% to 10%) is a solid, nonirritating option that absorbs oil, inhibits bacteria growth, calms redness, and mildly exfoliates. It’s a popular ingredient in spot-treatment products and face masks.

Best For: Blackheads, whiteheads, and mild red bumps on sensitive skin

Once available only by prescription, adapalene is a dermatologist favorite, even at its 0.1% OTC concentration. Like other topical retinoids such as retinol, which speed skin cell turnover and stimulate collagen production, it also has anti-inflammatory properties. (Bonus: As a vitamin A derivative, it can help reduce fine lines and wrinkles too.)

Best For: All types of acne on mature skin

Of all the alpha-hydroxy acids (AHAs) out there, glycolic acid can be a great option for someone who is acne prone, says Dr. Zeichner. Much like salicylic acid, glycolic acid removes dead skin cells and loosens excess sebum to de-gunk pores. Unlike salicylic acid (and other BHAs), it doesn’t get as deep into the pores. AHAs work primarily on the outer layers of the skin.

Try: Obagi Medical Clenziderm M.D. Resurfacing Multi-Acid Peel Pads

Severe acne like deep, painful nodules and cysts will almost always require help from a dermatologist. Depending on the type and severity of your breakouts, these are some of the most common solutions, which you’d pair with a simple at-home skin care routine (cleanser, moisturizer, and sunscreen).

A commonly prescribed topical Rx for comedonal acne (whiteheads and blackheads), this ingredient is a more potent vitamin A derivative than adapalene and could cause irritation. Most derms recommend starting slowly with a pea-size amount for your entire face once or twice a week and then gradually working up to nightly use.

Another first-line treatment are meds like doxycycline or minocycline, says dermatologist Kavita Mariwalla, MD. “These work by reducing bacterial counts in the pilosebaceous glands and are anti-inflammatory,” she explains. But they’re not a permanent solution: Dr. Mariwalla says most derms will keep patients on antibiotics temporarily (she reevaluates every eight weeks) until they begin to see results from their topical treatments (she often pairs antibiotics with topical tretinoin), which can take up to three months.

After antibiotics, this med (also known as Accutane) is the “next step up in the acne treatment pyramid,” says Dr. Mariwalla. It works by shrinking oil glands to reduce the likelihood of pore clogs that lead to acne. The most common side effects are dry skin, chapped lips, and dry eye. More serious issues, including a risk of birth defects and depression and anxiety, means your doctor will likely monitor you closely if you go this route. To mitigate these side effects, your doc may start you on a lower dose than what your weight would typically dictate—commonly known on TikTok as “microdosing Accutane.”

Spironolactone is one of the most common oral forms of these androgen-blocking medications and is typically reserved exclusively for women. (It can cause decreased libido and breast enlargement in men.) But Dr. Mariwalla says she’ll sometimes prescribe a topical version—another active called clascoterone, which goes by the brand name Winlevi—to treat red, inflamed bumps. It slows the skin’s natural oil production to help keep pimples from forming.

This oil-quelling FDA-cleared laser treatment—the first of its kind—offers an alternative to Accutane. It uses a precise wavelength of laser light to target sebum-producing glands. While effective, it can be pricey (up to $1,000 per treatment), requires several sessions, and may require maintenance treatments, Dr. Mariwalla says.

If you’ve got a whopper of a zit (a deep cyst or nodule) and a date or a big meeting to attend, dermatologists will sometimes inject cortisone to calm inflammation and quiet redness in a hurry. It’s important to note, however, that “this is a bandage, not a treatment for your acne,” says Dr. Mariwalla. “It’s effective for cysts but not acne that’s already been picked at.” If you don’t want to pay up to a couple hundred bucks for a shot (depending on your insurance), see the “Pimple Emergencies” box below.

Pimple Emergencies: Don’t squeeze! Apply a warm, damp washcloth for 10 to 15 minutes to help bring the pimple to the surface. Then apply a spot treatment with any of the OTC ingredients above. Do this daily until the pimple heals. If you’re not home, try rubbing some ice on it in 30-second intervals, then, if needed, apply a dash of 1% hydrocortisone cream to help with swelling and redness. (Pro tip: Keep this cream in your work bag to use sparingly and for no more than two to three days.) After, throw on a hydrocolloid pimple patch to aid healing or concealer to cover it.
